- On TCP, ECS instances from private images of client Windows (Windows 10, Windows 7) may be created only on a Dedicated Host (DeH). (KB-ZACD, docs, checked 2026-06-10)
- Sharing a private image between two tenants takes two calls in order: the owner POSTs the recipient project to /v1/cloudimages/members, then the recipient PUTs the same image with status accepted. The recipient then sees __imagetype=shared and can create servers from it, while only the owner may modify the image. (KB-ZC5V, live, checked 2026-07-21)
- IMS registers an image file already stored in OBS with POST /v2/cloudimages/action, taking name, image_url in bucket:object form, min_disk, os_type and is_config. The call returns a job_id polled at /v1/{project_id}/jobs/{job_id}, and entities.image_id appears about 15 seconds in, well before the job reaches SUCCESS. (KB-ZHKA, live, checked 2026-06-12)
- Image import takes files up to 128 GB in the supported formats. Between 128 GB and 1 TB only RAW with a bitmap file or ZVHD2 qualify, and 1 TB is the documented ceiling for image import. (KB-ZL7H, docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- Only unencrypted or SSE-KMS-encrypted external image files can be uploaded to OBS for an IMS import. (KB-ZOGU, docs, checked 2026-07-30)

## vpc (75)

- Once the WAF engine port is gone, the security group and the subnet delete cleanly. A VPC delete can still answer 409 VPC.0104 once while the subnet settles and then succeed on the next attempt. (KB-3TES, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- The VPC api-ref requires a plain IP address for the ip_address of an allowed address pair under the hardware SDN networking plan, and a dedicated security group for a pair whose mask is shorter than /24. (KB-3YKZ, docs, checked 2026-06-12)
- Updating an existing subnet with primary_dns and secondary_dns is rejected with VPC.0201 'secondary_dns fail when primary_dns is empty', although primary_dns is in the body. An update that sets the dnsList array returns 200, and running guests pick it up on DHCP renew. (KB-4AB2, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- TCP security group rules are not allow-only. Each rule has an action of allow or deny and a priority, read through the v3 API, and deny rules exist live in eu-de (KB-OVQP). The FAQ that calls security groups a whitelist describes allow rules only. (KB-4DQW, docs, checked 2026-10-02)
- The VPC quota and its used count for a project are returned by GET /v1/{project_id}/quotas with type vpc. (KB-4DTY, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- In the VPC API a subnet's id and its neutron_subnet_id are two different values; the subnet id equals its neutron_network_id. A port references both: its network_id holds the VPC subnet id and its fixed_ips subnet_id holds the neutron_subnet_id (neutron_subnet_id_v6 for an IPv6 address) (eu-de, checked 2026-09-25). (KB-4N3E, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In eu-de a single VPC is read with GET /v1/{project_id}/vpcs/{vpc_id}. The response wraps the object under the singular key vpc, while the collection call returns the list under vpcs. (KB-4NWB, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A subnet created over the API takes DNS server addresses and dhcp_enable as explicit fields. The values the API applies when those fields are omitted differ from the ones the console sets for a subnet. (KB-55UQ, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- The description of a security group rule is free text of up to 255 characters without angle brackets and is independent of the rule body. Whether a rule allows or denies is in its action field (allow or deny, default allow), which the v3 API returns and the v1 and v2.0 reads do not, so a rule read back as protocol null and remote_ip_prefix 0.0.0.0/0 matches all IPv4 traffic in its direction but is an allow-everything rule only if the v3 API shows action allow (eu-de, checked 2026-09-25). (KB-5TKG, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A VPC subnet in eu-de with no servers still carries two system ports in GET /v1/{project_id}/ports: device_owner network:dhcp with an empty device_id, and network:router_interface_distributed whose device_id is the VPC id. A check for leftovers before deleting a subnet can ignore those two owners. (KB-6FJO, live, checked 2026-10-05)
- A VPC subnet accepts masks down to /29: on 2026-09-25 in eu-de a /29 and a /28 subnet were created and turned ACTIVE. A /30 create answered HTTP 200, but the subnet never turned ACTIVE and was gone within a minute. The VPC user guide says /29, the API reference says the mask cannot be greater than 28, and the live result follows the user guide. (KB-7ISN, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- dhcp_enable has to be true on a VPC subnet for cloud-init in the guest to get an address and inject the SSH key. With it false the instance comes up without an address. (KB-7MAW, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- An unfiltered GET /v2.0/ports?limit=500 returned a body with no ports key at all. (KB-7NBH, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- Subnet delete uses the nested path DELETE /v1/{project_id}/vpcs/{vpc_id}/subnets/{subnet_id}, while create posts to the flat path /v1/{project_id}/subnets. (KB-7RKE, docs, checked 2026-08-18)
- Intra-subnet ICMP ping failed where the security group carried only a rule with no protocol set, while TCP over the same path worked. The VPC user manual lists All, TCP, UDP, GRE and ICMP as Protocol values. The observation and the manual disagree and no re-test has been made. (KB-ANXW, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A subnet created with POST /v1/{project_id}/subnets without DNS fields has an empty dnsList, so guests get no DNS resolvers: traffic by IP works, name resolution fails. The console fills in default resolvers, the API does not. Pass primary_dns and secondary_dns when creating subnets by API or IaC. (KB-BANM, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- Security group rules on TCP carry an action of allow or deny with a priority from 1 to 100, which only the v3 API returns. Traffic that matches no inbound rule is denied implicitly. Deny rules exist live in eu-de (KB-OVQP). Network ACL and firewall rules have their own allow and deny actions. (KB-BNGZ, docs, checked 2026-10-02)
- A VPC subnet gateway address is a settable field under Advanced Settings at subnet creation, where the .1 address is only the example. It is fixed once the subnet exists. (KB-BY5T, docs, checked 2026-08-26)
- The VPC user guide calls the subnet-level stateful allow and deny ACL a Firewall, while the VPC v3 API names the same object network_acl. (KB-BZZQ, docs, checked 2026-06-08)
- A VPC port has source and destination checking on by default, so an appliance routes or NATs traffic only after the check is disabled on every transit NIC, which is done by adding a wildcard allowed-address-pair entry to the port. (KB-C4X3, live, checked 2026-07-23)
- A VPC create is polled to status OK and a subnet create to status ACTIVE before the next resource in the chain is created. (KB-D2OW, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- In eu-de the VPC listing is GET /v1/{project_id}/vpcs and returns the networks under the response key vpcs. Each entry carries id, name, cidr and status. (KB-DR57, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- opentelekomcloud_vpc_subnet_v1 exposes two different ids: .id is the VPC subnet id, while .network_id is the value that NAT gateways, CCE, ELB, RDS and SFS expect. Passing the wrong one fails late and reads like a permission problem. (KB-DYU2, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- A single security group rule is removed with DELETE /v1/{project_id}/security-group-rules/{rule_id} on the VPC v1 API in eu-de, which answers HTTP 204. (KB-EVGE, live, checked 2026-06-08)
- When a VPN route for a CIDR already exists, creating a VPC subnet with that CIDR returns HTTP 200 and the route table then holds a local and an eni route for the same destination, with no error or warning. This contradicts the documented rule of one route per destination. (KB-F44Z, live, checked 2026-08-26)
- The VPC of a resource is read from a different field per service: ECS metadata.vpc_id, CCE spec.hostNetwork.vpc, ELB vpc_id, NAT gateway router_id, RDS vpc_id. An EIP is matched over its load balancer or over the private address inside the VPC CIDR. (KB-F5QT, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- VPC peering judges overlap on the declared VPC CIDR, not on the subnets in use. Two VPCs whose declared ranges overlap cannot be connected by a peering connection, and the documentation answer is to replan the network. (KB-FD3K, docs, checked 2026-09-16)
- VPC quota can be read before a create with GET /v1/{project_id}/quotas?type=vpc on vpc.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com. The 200 response carries quotas.resources[] entries with type, used, quota and min. (KB-GAQV, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- A newly created security group already carries an egress allow-all rule and an ingress rule for members of the same group. (KB-GRIL, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- Creating a VPC subnet whose CIDR duplicates an existing VPN route returns HTTP 200 in eu-de, and the route table then stably holds that destination twice, once as local and once as eni, although the documentation states duplicate destinations are rejected. (KB-GSLG, live, checked 2026-08-26)
- A VPC peering between two VPCs of the same tenant becomes active on creation, without a separate accept step. Routes have to be added on both sides, in Terraform with opentelekomcloud_vpc_route_v2, type "peering" and nexthop set to the peering id. (KB-H7KG, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- The VPC API reference on TCP exposes no sub-network-interface endpoints. The VLAN-based supplementary network interface capability exists on the platform but is consumed by the CCE Turbo CNI only, so there is no customer-usable equivalent of ENI prefix delegation on ECS. (KB-HLIZ, docs, checked 2026-06-12)
- Two VPC route conflicts are rejected: creating a VPN connection whose peer subnet equals an existing VPC subnet returns VPN.0219, and adding a peering route for a destination already routed to a VPN returns 400 DestinationAlreadyExist. (KB-HNE7, live, checked 2026-08-26)
- The VPC v1 API lists security groups together with their rules at /v1/{project_id}/security-groups in eu-de, returning security_groups[].security_group_rules[]. Each rule carries id, direction, ethertype as IPv4 or IPv6, protocol where null means any, port_range_min, port_range_max, remote_ip_prefix and remote_group_id. (KB-IJZM, live, checked 2026-06-08)
- GET /v1/{project_id}/subnets also returns legacy neutron networks, recognisable by status UNKNOWN, scope center and a missing vpc_id field. A record without vpc_id is not a VPC subnet. (KB-KGJY, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de network ACLs are read with the VPC v3 call GET /v3/{project_id}/vpc/firewalls, which returns HTTP 200 with the response keys firewalls and page_info. (KB-KMWL, live, checked 2026-07-02)
- The VPC quota of a project is read with GET /v1/{project_id}/quotas against the VPC endpoint in eu-de. The response carries one record per type, for example type vpc with a used and a quota value. (KB-KQ3Z, live, checked 2026-08-26)
- A subnet created with POST /v1/{project_id}/subnets and no DNS fields ends up with an empty dnsList, and its instances get no resolver. The console fills in the regional resolver addresses, the API does not. (KB-KS3J, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- A port whose device_owner still points at an already deleted VM blocks deletion of its subnet and VPC, and a tenant cannot remove such a port itself. Clearing it required a support ticket to force-delete the stale port or the VPC. (KB-KZJU, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A VPC subnet is created with dhcp_enable true by default. (KB-L6FG, docs, checked 2026-08-18)
- A security group rule is added with POST /v1/{project_id}/security-group-rules and removed individually by rule id with DELETE on the same path. A newly added rule takes a few seconds to become effective. (KB-LERK, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- In allowed_address_pairs a pair whose prefix is /0 with any non-zero address is accepted as the any-source wildcard, while a literal 0.0.0.0/0 is rejected by request validation. (KB-LSXG, live, checked 2026-07-23)
- In a bridge VPC, /32 routes to each source database address over cross-tenant VPC peering plus one /24 route to the destination VPCs over intra-tenant peering carry DRS traffic in both directions. Longest prefix match decides, and DRS replication instances receive addresses different from the source database. (KB-LTUU, live, checked 2026-02-04)
- After a persistent connection is cut, a new connection under the new rule only forms once the connection tracking entry has timed out: 600 seconds for established TCP, 30 seconds for TCP in FIN-WAIT and for ICMP, 180 seconds for other protocols with traffic in both directions and 30 seconds with traffic in one direction. (KB-LUT7, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Adding a VPC peering route for a destination already covered by a VPN route returns 400 DestinationAlreadyExist. (KB-LXWR, live, checked 2026-08-26)
- POST /v1/{project_id}/subnets leaves primary_dns and secondary_dns empty when the fields are omitted, while the console form prefills them. An instance on such a subnet reaches ACTIVE and answers SSH, and name resolution in the guest fails. (KB-MM4Z, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- A security group rule description is free text and does not define what the rule does: a rule with protocol null, no ports and a remote prefix covering all addresses matches all traffic in its direction and allows it only when its action is allow. The action (allow or deny, with a priority from 1 to 100) is returned by the v3 API but not by the v1 and v2.0 reads, so audits must read action, priority, protocol and remote_ip_prefix from the v3 API, never the description (eu-de, checked 2026-09-25). (KB-MOMQ, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A security group filters inbound traffic to an ECS while egress is open by default, so an outbound transfer from the instance to an external host over SSH runs without any security group change. (KB-MQP4, live, checked 2026-06-16)
- Subnets set up through the console in eu-de carry primary_dns 100.125.4.25 and secondary_dns 100.125.129.199, the platform resolvers. A subnet created through the API or Terraform should set both explicitly. (KB-N2BY, live, checked 2026-10-05)
- VPC firewall rule changes never cut existing connections: adding, changing or deleting a firewall rule and associating or disassociating a subnet leave all inbound and outbound persistent connections up. The new rules apply to new connections only. (KB-N6UD, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Every VPC route table carries system routes for 100.64.0.0/10 and 198.19.128.0/20 and a loopback route for 127.0.0.0/8. System routes cannot be modified or deleted. (KB-NLXW, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The default VPC quota per tenant is 10, and creating an eleventh VPC returns VPC.0114 OverQuota. Current use is readable with GET /v1/{project_id}/quotas, where the subnet quota is 100. Raising the VPC quota goes through a support request. (KB-NPZK, live, checked 2026-07-23)
- Traffic over an active VPC peering needs a route entry in the route table on each of the two sides, with the peer VPC CIDR, a peer subnet CIDR or a peer ECS IP address as destination. (KB-O6IU, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Security group rules on TCP are not allow-only. The v3 API and the console carry an action of allow or deny and a priority, and deny rules exist live in eu-de (KB-OVQP). The older FAQ wording on a whitelist mechanism and aggregated rules predates this and does not cover deny rules. Restricting egress can use a deny rule in the security group or another control such as Cloud Firewall, NAT or routes. (KB-OIUA, docs, checked 2026-10-02)
- A VPC peering whose two VPCs belong to different tenants becomes active only after the owner of the accepting VPC accepts the request. (KB-OOK5, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- TCP security groups can hold deny rules. A live read of the v3 security group rules API in eu-de on 2026-10-02 returned two enabled ingress rules with action deny on one test tenant (priority 4 and 5, IPv4, all protocols) next to allow rules. The other test tenant held 205 rules, all allow. The v3 API returns action and priority on every rule. (KB-OVQP, live, checked 2026-10-02)
- The allow-any rule that references a security group's own id appears twice in a listing, once with ethertype IPv4 and once with IPv6. The two entries form one pair covering both address families, not a duplicated rule. (KB-PF6Q, live, checked 2026-06-08)
- Security groups track connections: a changed inbound rule applies to existing traffic at once, while outbound changes only affect new connections. Any added, changed or deleted rule and any instance added to or removed from the group clears the inbound persistent connections of all instances in the group, while outbound persistent connections stay. (KB-PI2D, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- VPC peering connections are listed with GET /v2.0/vpc/peerings, a v2.0 path that carries no project id segment. The response key is peerings and each entry has name, status, request_vpc_info with vpc_id, and accept_vpc_info with vpc_id and tenant_id. (KB-PWJP, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- POST /v1/{project_id}/subnets stores primary_dns and secondary_dns as empty when the request omits them. The console prefills both fields, the API does not, so a subnet built by API or Terraform can hand out no resolver at all. The region resolver addresses have to be set explicitly. (KB-Q5W2, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- In eu-de two subnets deleted with DELETE /v1/{project_id}/vpcs/{vpc_id}/subnets/{subnet_id} were gone within seconds, their dhcp and router-interface ports included, and the VPC could be deleted right after. A GET on the deleted VPC answers 404 VPC.0105 with the Neutron message RouterNotFound, so a VPC is a Neutron router underneath. (KB-RD6U, live, checked 2026-10-05)
- GET /v2.0/ports?network_id={id} returned 0 ports for a subnet that held three ports, one of them attached to a running VM. The same call without the filter, matched client-side on network_id and on fixed_ips subnet_id, returned all three. (KB-SLWK, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- Virtual Private Cloud (VPC) provides isolated virtual networks inside a tenant: each VPC carries its own IP address ranges, subnets, virtual routers, security groups and network ACLs, and the customer can create one or more VPCs. VPC flow logs capture the traffic of the network interfaces of generation 7 and generation 9 servers and forward it to Log Tank Service for storage and analysis. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for VPC in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-UTUP, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- The resource opentelekomcloud_vpc_secgroup_v3 creates the IPv4 and IPv6 allow-all egress rules by itself. Declaring an additional catch-all egress rule on the same security group returns 409 VPC.9902 "Security group rule already exists" and stops the apply. (KB-VBVB, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- GET /v1/{project_id}/quotas returns the per-resource quotas of a project, including the VPC quota. On a test tenant in eu-de the call reported the VPC quota at 10 of 10 used. (KB-VCIT, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de a subnet object returned by the VPC v1 API can carry an optional availability_zone attribute (the AZ specified at creation) and omits the key when none is set (checked 2026-09-25). The value does not limit where the subnet can be used: a subnet is defined by its VPC and its CIDR range and is not bound to a single availability zone. (KB-VLGS, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Anti-spoofing on a VPC NIC drops packets whose source or destination address differs from the NIC's fixed IP, which affects any routing, firewall or VPN gateway instance. It is opened per NIC with PUT /v2.0/ports/{port_id} on vpc.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com setting allowed_address_pairs, returning HTTP 200. (KB-W7H2, live, checked 2026-07-23)
- In eu-de the quota for type vpc returned by the VPC quota endpoint is 10 VPCs per project. (KB-WGTH, live, checked 2026-08-26)
- Subnets are AZ-agnostic: an ECS placed in eu-de-03 was created on a subnet labelled eu-de-01 without problems. (KB-XGKU, live, checked 2026-07-20)
- A VPC create in a project that has reached its VPC quota fails with a quota error, not with a syntax or validation error. (KB-Y72H, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- The description of a security group rule is free text and has no effect on filtering. A rule described "Deny all traffic" with protocol All, port range 0 to 0 and remote_ip_prefix 0.0.0.0/0 is an ordinary allow-all rule. (KB-YGSM, docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- A VPC route table on T Cloud Public (TCP) cannot hold two routes with the same destination, even when the next hop types differ, and the system route created for a VPC subnet CIDR cannot be modified or deleted. (KB-YHLQ, docs, checked 2026-08-26)
- VPC peering is not billed. (KB-YHSH, contract, checked 2026-09-16)
- The default VPC route table quota is 200 routes per table, which caps a static-routing pod network on ECS at roughly 200 nodes before a quota increase. (KB-ZKI3, docs, checked 2026-06-12)
- In the opentelekomcloud Terraform provider 1.37.8, opentelekomcloud_vpc_secgroup_rule_v3 uses the argument names ether_type and multi_port, carries an explicit action field, and takes a port list or a range in one field. The v2 spellings ethertype and multiport are rejected by terraform validate, and port_range_min and port_range_max are absent from the v3 schema. (KB-ZULT, live, checked 2026-09-16)

## elb (49)

- Creating the pool with listener_id and leaving default_pool_id off the listener makes the pool depend on the listener in Terraform, so destroy removes the pool first and the listener second, matching the order the ELB v3 API accepts. (KB-2BL5, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- Only a dedicated load balancer can send traffic to servers outside its own VPC. With IP as a backend enabled it adds servers by private IPv4 address in a VPC linked by VPC peering or in an on-premises data centre linked by Direct Connect or VPN. (KB-36IY, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A server added to a dedicated load balancer by its IP address takes at most 50,000 concurrent connections. (KB-3LFE, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Load balancers are listed with GET /v2/{project_id}/elb/loadbalancers. The response holds loadbalancers[], and each entry carries name, id, operating_status, provisioning_status and vip_address. (KB-4XYI, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/loadbalancers/{loadbalancer_id} for a dedicated load balancer that no longer exists answers HTTP 404 with error_code ELB.8904, 'loadbalancer <id> could not be found'. Seen after a terraform destroy that stopped halfway: the load balancer was gone in TCP while the Terraform state still listed it. (KB-7QDZ, live, checked 2026-10-06)
- The public price API carries a record named Shared Elastic Loadbalancer for eu-nl (seen on 2026-09-25), although section 6.5.3 of the service description shows n.a. for EU-NL and the ELB documentation allows only dedicated load balancers in eu-nl, so that record is no proof of availability. (KB-B4SW, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- When an elastic IP is passed into opentelekomcloud_lb_loadbalancer_v3 by id, the attribute public_ip[0].address stays null, so an output that reads it fails the apply at the last step with every resource already created. (KB-BETP, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- IP as a backend cannot be switched off once it is enabled. In the v3 API the load balancer field ip_target_enable can only be updated to true. (KB-BFDT, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- An ELB HTTPS listener presents a server certificate whose content and private key the owner uploads in PEM format, and with SNI one listener serves several domain names with their own certificates, so the TLS handshake shows the owner's domain and certificate. (KB-BNR5, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Servers added by IP address cannot receive the client source IP address. For HTTP and HTTPS listeners the ELB guide points to the X-Forwarded-For header instead and it names no alternative for TCP and UDP listeners. (KB-CJTJ, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On 2026-09-25 GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/loadbalancers on elb.eu-de returned the field ip_target_enable in the load balancer object, so the state of IP as a backend can be read back through the API. (KB-CQMN, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- DELETE /v3/{project_id}/elb/pools/{id} returns HTTP 204 while the listener that the pool belongs to still exists, which frees the listener for a later delete. (KB-DIPH, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- A shared load balancer cannot route traffic to backend servers in another VPC and cannot be used across accounts. The shared ELB API requires each member to sit in a subnet of the VPC of the load balancer. (KB-DVMF, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In the opentelekomcloud provider a changed certificate body on an ELB v3 certificate resource is planned as "updated in-place": the plan carries no replacement marker, and an apply would swap the certificate the listener serves. (KB-DWDN, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- On 2026-10-05 GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/flavors in eu-de listed six dedicated L7 flavors with their QPS: L7_flavor.elb.s1.small 4,000, s1.medium 16,000, s1.large 80,000, s2.small 8,000, s2.medium 40,000 and s2.large 160,000, plus L4 flavors s1 and s2 in small, medium and large. Terraform's l7_flavor takes the flavor id, which the data source opentelekomcloud_lb_flavor_v3 finds by name. (KB-E2KU, live, checked 2026-10-05)
- A shared ELB v2.0 in eu-de with an HTTP listener on port 80, a ROUND_ROBIN pool and an HTTP health monitor marked an ECS backend ONLINE within about 20 s of the monitor create, once the web server on the backend answered. The backend security group let port 80 in from the subnet range and from 100.125.0.0/16; which of the two the health checks use was not proven. (KB-EGHR, live, checked 2026-10-12)
- The shared ELB v2.0 API in eu-de accepts AK/SK calls signed SDK-HMAC-SHA256 without an X-Project-Id header: the creates of load balancer, listener, pool, member and health monitor answered 201, the deletes 204, and a read after the delete answered 404. (KB-ERDD, live, checked 2026-10-12)
- A backend member of a dedicated load balancer in eu-de that has nothing listening yet shows operating_status OFFLINE with reason_code CONNECT_FAILED in GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/pools/{pool_id}/members, and the HTTPS listener then answers 502. In a run on 2026-10-06 the server was created at 12:43 UTC, cloud-init reached modules:final at 12:44:51, nginx was being installed at about 12:45:06 and the member read ONLINE with the site answering 200 at 12:45:28: about two and a half minutes from terraform creating the server to a working site. (KB-ESMU, live, checked 2026-10-06)
- The ELB v3 certificate create at POST /v3/{project_id}/elb/certificates accepts both RSA and ECDSA certificates. (KB-FA4K, live, checked 2026-05-26)
- DELETE /v3/{project_id}/elb/listeners/{listener_id} on a dedicated load balancer answers 409 ELB.8907 'pool <id> is using this listener' while a pool is bound to the listener, also when the pool has no members left. Deleting the pool first frees the listener. In Terraform, bind the pool with listener_id on the pool resource instead of default_pool_id on the listener: then destroy deletes the pool before the listener; with default_pool_id it tries the listener first and stops. (KB-GTIT, live, checked 2026-10-06)
- A dedicated load balancer binds an existing elastic IP through public_ip { id = ... } and an SNAT rule binds one through floating_ip_id, so addresses allocated in a separate stack keep their ids and their DNS record across destroy and re-apply of the consuming stack. (KB-GZM3, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- Shared load balancers cannot be created in eu-nl. Only dedicated load balancers can be created there, on the console or through the API. (KB-H34Q, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) offers load balancing at layer 4 and layer 7 through Elastic Load Balancer (ELB): network load balancing for TCP and UDP and application load balancing for HTTP and HTTPS, as shared or dedicated load balancers, with dedicated ones spread over up to three availability zones. (KB-HZXL, docs, checked 2026-10-11)
- L7 rules of a policy are listed with GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/l7policies/{policy_id}/rules. Each rule carries id, type such as PATH, HOST_NAME or HEADER, compare_type such as STARTS_WITH or EQUAL_TO, value, conditions and invert. (KB-ICS6, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On 2026-09-25 GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/flavors in eu-de returned 17 dedicated load balancer flavors over five types: L4 (6), L7 (6), L7_elastic (3), L4_elastic_max (1) and L7_elastic_max (1). This is what the listing showed on that date. (KB-J3DZ, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A dedicated ELB v3 load balancer create expects vip_subnet_cidr_id to hold the neutron subnet id; with the VPC subnet id the VIP is not placed. The mapping comes from GET /v1/{project_id}/subnets/{vpc_subnet_id}, whose response carries neutron_subnet_id. (KB-KG3S, live, checked 2026-05-26)
- A dedicated load balancer sends its health checks, and for HTTP or HTTPS listeners the forwarded traffic, from IP addresses in its own backend subnet, so the backend servers' security group must allow that subnet on the service and health check ports. A shared load balancer uses 100.125.0.0/16 instead. (KB-KHNT, docs, checked 2026-10-06)
- ELB v3 returns l4_flavor_id and l7_flavor_id as UUIDs, resolved through GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/flavors, which returned 17 records on 2026-09-17 with names of the form L4_flavor.elb.s1.small and L7_flavor.elb.s1.small. (KB-KK7Q, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- Through the v3 API such a server is added as a member with a private IPv4 address and without subnet_cidr_id. The API reference allows it only in backend server groups with protocol TCP, HTTP or HTTPS (the console guide also names UDP) and answers ELB.8939 while ip_target_enable is false. (KB-KKT4, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The ELB v3 API rejects deletion of a listener while a pool still points at it, with 409 ELB.8907 "pool <id> is using this listener". The stack applies and serves traffic, the failure appears at teardown. (KB-KZUN, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- The ELB FAQ and the v3 API also allow IP backends in a VPC of another region that is linked by a Cloud Connect cloud connection. Cloud Connect is listed under preview and beta versions in the service description, section 3.12.7. (KB-LD5O, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- For an L7 rule created with conditions and no top-level value, the v3 API returns value as an empty string and the match values in the conditions array, one per entry, in an order of its own: two conditions came back reversed on 2026-09-25. A rule created with a value and conditions returns that value. (KB-LJKN, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In the opentelekomcloud Terraform provider an HTTPS listener takes its server certificate through default_tls_container_ref, which must hold the id of an opentelekomcloud_lb_certificate_v3 resource created with the fields certificate, private_key and domain. TLS terminates at the load balancer listener. (KB-MPJZ, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- The v3 rule listing and the v3 single-rule-by-id call return identical rule data for an L7 rule, conditions included. (KB-NAR6, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de the ELB v3 call GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/certificates returns HTTP 200 and delivers the certificate list under the response key certificates. (KB-OACX, live, checked 2026-07-02)
- Security group rules of servers added by IP address must allow traffic from the backend subnet of the load balancer, otherwise health checks fail. The ELB FAQ asks for at least 16 free IP addresses in the subnet where the load balancer resides to enable IP as a backend. (KB-OEB6, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A dedicated load balancer placed in eu-de-01 or eu-de-02 reaches backend servers sitting in eu-de-03 across availability zones inside the same VPC. (KB-OVNG, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Rebuilding an ELB in another project includes creating the backend pools and their members again. (KB-OVTY, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- ELB L7 policies are listed with GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/l7policies using limit (default and maximum 2000) and marker paging; the response holds l7policies[] plus page_info with current_count and previous_marker; next_marker appears only when the server offers another page. In eu-de on 2026-09-25 a short page did not mean the end of the list: with limit=2 the first page held 1 of 3 policies and carried no next_marker, while the default limit returned all 3. (KB-P5PW, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- POST /v3/{project_id}/elb/certificates expects the body wrapped in a certificate object. A flat body returns HTTP 400 "Invalid request body: Error map format". The inner fields are name, type (server), domain, certificate and private_key. (KB-PJVB, live, checked 2026-05-26)
- GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/availability-zones on eu-de returned eu-de-01 and eu-de-02 on two separate tenants. The service description states up to three availability zones, which is a contractual maximum and not the zone list this endpoint returned. (KB-QQBS, live, checked 2026-10-05)
- A forwarding policy of a dedicated ELB takes one rule per rule type: on 2026-09-25 a second PATH rule on the same policy was refused with HTTP 409 ELB.8907 (already has rules with type PATH), while a HOST_NAME rule next to it was accepted. (KB-ROUV, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On 2026-07-02 GET /v3/{project_id}/elb/availability-zones in eu-de returned exactly two zones, eu-de-01 and eu-de-02, both in state ACTIVE. Dedicated load balancer placement on that date followed this listing. (KB-SBUW, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Each L7 policy returned by the v3 listing carries id, name, action, listener_id, redirect_pool_id, redirect_url_config and priority. (KB-TQG5, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A TERMINATED_HTTPS listener on ELB requires the TLS certificate to be uploaded into the target project before the listener is created. (KB-U47A, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The resource opentelekomcloud_lb_loadbalancer_v3 exposes the public address of a dedicated load balancer as public_ip[0].address. (KB-YFA5, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- For multi-condition L7 rules the v2 rules endpoint returns an empty value string and its response body carries no conditions array, so the match value of such a rule is not present in the v2 response; the v3 response carries it in conditions. (KB-YNV3, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Dedicated load balancers and public NAT gateways are offered in eu-nl. On 2026-09-25 the ELB v3 flavor list of eu-nl returned the same 17 dedicated flavors as eu-de, the ELB AZ list returned eu-nl-01, eu-nl-02 and eu-nl-03, and the public NAT API (GET /v2.0/nat_gateways) answered in eu-nl. (KB-ZABN, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Elastic Load Balancer (ELB) distributes incoming traffic across several backend servers, checks their health and works with Auto Scaling. It is offered as shared and as dedicated load balancers for application load balancing at layer 7 and network load balancing at layer 4; a dedicated load balancer has its own resources, can span up to three availability zones and comes in fixed and elastic flavors. The documentation states that eu-de offers dedicated and shared load balancers and eu-nl offers dedicated ones. (KB-ZYJM, contract, checked 2026-10-11)

## cce (40)

- Cloud Container Engine and Cloud Container Engine Turbo (CCE, CCE Turbo) provide managed Kubernetes clusters in which the customer runs, manages and monitors containers, with the compute, storage and network resources reserved on T Cloud Public (TCP). CCE Turbo adds passthrough networking (Cloud Native 2.0) and better scheduling compared with CCE Standard; the preassembled cluster flavors hold up to 50, 200, 1,000 or 2,000 nodes, among them a high-availability flavor. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for CCE in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-22XD, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- A new CCE cluster comes with 17 storage classes, among them `csi-disk`, `csi-nas`, `csi-obs`, `csi-sfsturbo` and older flexvolume ones, and none of them carries the `storageclass.kubernetes.io/is-default-class` annotation. A PersistentVolumeClaim that does not name a class stays Pending. (KB-27TG, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- CCE Turbo clusters (Cloud Native Network 2.0) are documented and orderable on TCP through cluster category Turbo, containerNetwork mode eni and an eniNetwork block in the create-cluster API, and add per-pod security groups and ELB passthrough. (KB-2HZA, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- GP SSD v2 (GPSSD2) is valid for CCE persistent volumes through the everest CSI driver on TCP, eu-de, and is set in the StorageClass. (KB-3DQF, docs, checked 2026-09-09)
- A live price API query for cce returned only s1 and s2 cluster management items, with no line for the Turbo variant. (KB-3UFL, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- CCE publishes https://<cluster-uid>.cce.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com in status.endpoints as external_otc. The name resolves to the platform API gateway and serves a valid certificate even when no elastic IP is bound to the cluster API. (KB-4Z2W, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- GET /api/v3/clusterupgradepaths on the CCE endpoint needs no cluster id and returns the upgrade targets per cluster version and platform version, a call the CCE API reference in the docs mirror does not list. On 2026-09-25 it offered v1.30 clusters on platform version cce.14.70 or cce.14.72 an upgrade to v1.31.14-r32, and older v1.30 platform versions only the patch v1.30.14-r72. (KB-5BJH, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- CCE nodes join a cluster with no internet egress, because bootstrap and add-ons come from internal endpoints. A pod pulling an image from a public registry then stays in ImagePullBackOff. One NAT gateway with an SNAT rule covers the whole node subnet. (KB-5IOB, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- The CCE Kubernetes version policy in the docs mirror (commit of 2026-08-31) lists v1.30 to v1.34 as in commercial use, with end of service in November 2026 for v1.30, June 2027 for v1.31, October 2027 for v1.32, March 2028 for v1.33 and July 2028 for v1.34. v1.29 reached end of service in June 2026. (KB-5TID, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A CCE cluster with container_network_type overlay_l2 rejects an Ubuntu 22.04 node pool on versions v1.31 through v1.34 with 403 CCE.01403001, "Not support Ubuntu 22.04 in overlay_l2 cluster". EulerOS 2.9 and HCE OS 2.0 are accepted there, spelled that way in the API body. (KB-6O6J, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- On a CCE worker the kubelet reports `nodefs` of about 10 GiB, with allocatable ephemeral-storage 9.8 GiB, even on a 50 GiB system disk. Images and container layers sit on the data disk as `imagefs`, 88 GiB on a 100 GiB volume. Everything under /var/lib/kubelet, including every emptyDir, uses the small nodefs. (KB-75DH, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- In a CCE cluster with the VPC or tunnel network, traffic from a pod to an external overlay relay leaves through the node, so a relay port such as UDP 9993 for ZeroTier must be allowed in the node security group only if its default allow-all outbound rule was hardened. In a CCE Turbo cluster the ENI security group governs pod egress instead. (KB-76SZ, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET /api/v3/projects/{project_id}/clusters returns the CCE clusters of a project and answers with an empty list when none exist. (KB-7CH3, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- The Python kubernetes client method CoreV1Api.read_namespaced_pod_log with the default _preload_content=True returns a str holding the repr of the response bytes (b'...\n...'). Passing _preload_content=False and decoding resp.data gives the raw log text. (KB-AUZM, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- On 2026-09-25 the CCE upgrade path API listed cluster version v1.35 (latest patch v1.35.3-r4) in eu-de and eu-nl, and most CCE add-on templates declared support for v1.35, although the version policy in the docs mirror ends at v1.34. No v1.34 platform version had v1.35 as an upgrade target yet. (KB-BB4E, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- ECS instances serving as CCE worker nodes carry the string tags CCE-Cluster-ID and CCE-Dynamic-Provisioning-Node. The CCE node field status.privateIP equals the Kubernetes node name, which joins the ECS view to the CCE view. (KB-BHHR, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- The node security group that CCE creates already allows the node port range 30000-32767, so the load balancer reaches a NodePort Service over the node address with the default rules. (KB-C5QV, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- A managed CCE cluster can have worker nodes with no SSH access. Host-level software is then installed from inside the cluster by a privileged pod that runs in the host namespaces. (KB-CGCI, live, checked 2026-02-04)
- On CCE a 10 Gi PersistentVolumeClaim with the csi-disk storage class bound in about one minute. While the volume is being provisioned, exec into the pod answers "container not found". (KB-CR6R, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- CCE creates the SWR pull credential default-secret in every namespace of a cluster, so a Pod or Job pulling a private image from SWR only needs imagePullSecrets: [{name: default-secret}]. (KB-E6ID, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- CCE Turbo node pools accept x86 VM flavors from the c3n, c4ne, c7n, c9, x2ne, s7n, s9, m3n, m7n and m9 families plus the GPU families g7, pi5e and p5e, as the CCE API reference lists them on 2026-09-25; region availability varies. (KB-GMRI, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET /api/v3/addontemplates on the CCE endpoint returned 14 add-on templates on eu-de on 2026-09-25: autoscaler, cce-hpa-controller, metrics-server, nginx-ingress, gpu-beta, volcano, coredns, everest, grafana, log-agent, npd, storage-driver, node-local-dns and cie-collector. The newest supportVersions patterns reach cluster version v1.35. (KB-I7J7, live, checked 2026-10-05)
- On CCE, `csi-disk` is the EVS-backed block storage class, and a 10Gi PersistentVolumeClaim naming it bound in about 5 seconds. (KB-JGA2, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- While no elastic IP is bound to the CCE cluster API, the external_otc endpoint cannot be authenticated against, although it resolves and serves a valid certificate. (KB-KTT2, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- Pod density on a CCE Turbo node is capped by the node flavor's supplementary network interface quota rather than by the kubelet default. Documentation examples give 128 pods on a 4xlarge class node and 256 on an 8xlarge class node. (KB-NFSU, docs, checked 2026-06-12)
- Application Service Mesh (ASM) is a managed service mesh compatible with Kubernetes and Istio that manages the lifecycle and traffic of microservices without changes to the application. It provides load balancing, outlier detection and fault injection, and grayscale releases such as canary and blue-green deployment. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the ASM API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-NMSH, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- GET /api/v3/projects/{project_id}/clusters on the CCE endpoint answers HTTP 200 with a payload of kind Cluster, apiVersion v3 and an items array. The call is AK/SK signed like other platform APIs. (KB-NOWE, live, checked 2026-07-02)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) offers managed Kubernetes in Cloud Container Engine (CCE), as CCE Standard and CCE Turbo clusters, with Kubernetes v1.30 to v1.34 in commercial use per the version policy in the documentation mirror of 2026-08-31 (KB-5TID). Clusters use ECS nodes, VPC, ELB and EVS, SFS or OBS storage, and container images come from Software Repository for Containers. (KB-OOVV, docs, checked 2026-10-11)
- Ubiquitous Cloud Native Service (UCS) is a platform that centrally manages distributed Kubernetes clusters, so cloud-native applications run across regions and clouds, on CCE and CCE Turbo clusters of T Cloud Public (TCP) as well as on Kubernetes clusters of other cloud vendors, with traffic distribution between them. The service description of 17.08.2026 announced it as expected to be available from 1 September 2026. (KB-P3JV, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- When concurrent emptyDir-heavy pods fill nodefs on a CCE worker, the node gets `node.kubernetes.io/disk-pressure` and further pods stay Pending with an untolerated taint. `GET /api/v1/nodes/<node>/proxy/stats/summary` can report 5 percent used while the taint is still set, because the kubelet holds the condition for its transition period. (KB-QYGN, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- On a standard CCE cluster a LoadBalancer ingress registers only Services of type NodePort. With a ClusterIP backend the controller logs `can not found protocol port of pod <ns>/<pod>, skip add member`, the ELB pool stays empty and requests return 502. ClusterIP backends work on a CCE Turbo cluster with a dedicated load balancer. (KB-SH6U, docs, checked 2026-09-16)
- The public price API carries the CCE cluster charge under sn=cce as one hourly item per cluster flavor: cce.s1.small, cce.s1.medium and, for the HA clusters, cce.s2.small to cce.s2.xlarge, six items in eu-de on 2026-09-24. Worker nodes, EIP, NAT gateway and EVS are priced under their own service names. (KB-SJJV, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- The CCE add-on catalog read on eu-de on 2026-09-25 held no keda and no knative template, so on that date no add-on offered request driven pod scale to zero. (KB-SQ4W, live, checked 2026-10-05)
- The number of master nodes in a CCE cluster is fixed when the cluster is created, so a highly available control plane has to be chosen at creation time. (KB-UOZO, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On CCE the number of master nodes is fixed at cluster creation. Changing the cluster scale (50, 200, 1000 nodes) leaves the master count unchanged and only moves upward, so reaching a three-master HA cluster from a single-master one means a new cluster. (KB-VXRJ, docs, checked 2026-09-09)
- A stale `node.kubernetes.io/disk-pressure` taint is removed with `kubectl taint node <node> node.kubernetes.io/disk-pressure-`; Kubernetes adds it again automatically while the node disk space is still near its limit. (KB-WJEE, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The hashicorp/kubernetes provider for a CCE cluster must be configured from state written by an earlier apply, read through terraform_remote_state plus base64decode of certificate_clusters and certificate_users from opentelekomcloud_cce_cluster_v3. Providers are configured before resources created in the same apply exist. (KB-WPTN, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- The CCE version policy says the console creates clusters only of the latest two commercial versions (v1.33 and v1.34) and that v1.30, v1.31 and v1.32 clusters are created only through the API, while the create-cluster API reference says the console offers the latest three versions. (KB-XVNK, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- When a CCE cluster version reaches end of service, CCE stops creating new clusters of that version and ends technical support, vulnerability fixes, patches and the CCE SLA for clusters that still run it. (KB-Z6PU, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET /api/v3/projects/{project_id}/quotas on cce.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com returns the CCE quota. On a test project the read showed a limit of 5 clusters per region on that date. (KB-ZZK5, live, checked 2026-08-18)

## pricing (39)

- The price API returns rows for flavors marked sellout in cond:operation:az and for ramped-down services. Orderability follows from the per-AZ state, not from the presence of a price row. (KB-4ABK, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- No public service description of the Swiss offering (eu-ch2) was found on 2026-10-08: the TCP service description revision 17.08.2026 covers EU-DE and EU-NL only, the Swiss product pages link that same general document, and the operator's Swiss site offers a price calculator in CHF and a technical flyer of 2022 but no service description or price list. What is offered in eu-ch2 has to be confirmed with the Swiss entity before it is quoted. (KB-4FW6, docs, checked 2026-10-08)
- Answers of the public price API can carry response.cachedAt, the time of a server cache in server time, two hours ahead of UTC on 2026-09-24. That day an unfiltered query answered from a cache of 2026-09-07 and a query for sn=ecs from one of 2026-09-17, so a price read today can come from a cache more than two weeks old. (KB-5LLN, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- A price API result for one sn can contain records of another product: the sn=dc result for rn=eu-de carried one stray DAS row, distinguishable by its productName field. (KB-6A7H, live, checked 2026-08-25)
- In the c9 price table of the TCP service description the flavour name is printed on the Oracle row, and the open Linux row directly above it carries its rate without a flavour name. A search combining a c9 flavour name with the words open Linux matches nothing. (KB-6HHT, contract, checked 2026-09-11)
- The TCP price API returns rows for services that are no longer offerable. By 2026-09-02 this had been observed for SFS Common I/O, BMS and DIS, so a price-API hit alone does not show that a service can be ordered. (KB-7VPP, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- Every record of the public price API has an id, a SKU code, while opiFlavour is empty on some: in eu-de on 2026-09-24 on 11 of 461 rds records, 1 of 11 eip records and 6 of 27 coss records. Every ecs, deh, obs, elb and nat record carried one. The id is the key to look a record up by. (KB-AEVL, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- In the TCP service description the commercial limit per business partner (section 3.1.1.1, footnote 2) and the footnote 1 marker for no guaranteed capacity are set only on the flexible-purpose families x1, x1e, x1m, x2, x2e, x2m and x2ne. The s9, m9 and c9 rows carry neither marker. (KB-AH5P, contract, checked 2026-09-11)
- The price API covers the s2, s3, s7n and s9 ECS families, so a flavor missing from the price API can still be listed in the service description catalog. (KB-AI2E, live, checked 2026-07-15)
- A query of the price API that matches nothing answers HTTP 200 with stats.count 0 and result as an empty list, while a query that matches answers result as an object keyed by service name. A service name the API does not know answers HTTP 500 with code Error, so an empty answer and a refused query can be told apart. (KB-BVUH, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- A price change is pre-announced inside the current revision of the service description. A section 6 price row can carry a "from DD.MM.YYYY:" subline: the value under the marker is the new hourly price, the value on the row above is the one in force until that date. (KB-FBVR, contract, checked 2026-07-05)
- Money values returned by the public price API use English number formatting: comma as thousands separator, dot as decimal separator, six decimal places, and the currency code appended after the value. (KB-FE6O, live, checked 2026-06-11)
- Windows ECS are priced in a separate, higher tier per flavor than the Open Linux tier: on 2026-09-24 the Windows rate was higher on all 306 flavors of the six ECS service names in eu-de that carry both. The OS of every VM decides which price row applies. (KB-FIBF, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- Price rows in the service description can carry a "from <date>:" subline that announces a future value, so a parser reading only the main row returns the superseded value. (KB-FZRN, contract, checked 2026-07-01)
- The en variant of the price API writes every amount as a number with a decimal point, a comma as thousands separator and the currency code after it, while the de variant writes a decimal comma. A reader has to know which variant it reads, because a comma followed by three digits is ambiguous on its own. (KB-H5VQ, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- A pre-announced price change can move different rows by different ratios. In the change effective 01.07.2026, 81 of 90 affected ECS price points moved by factor 1.2500 and the nine s9 Windows rows by 1.1103, the compute and the Windows license component moving separately. (KB-HOSK, contract, checked 2026-07-05)
- A query to the TCP price API with sn=ucs returned HTTP 500 rather than an empty result set. (KB-HSN2, live, checked 2026-09-01)
- Reserved terms and discount depth are family-dependent: the 36-month term is offered for part of the ECS families. (KB-HSTO, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The unit field of the price API is free text with about 20 values, among them h, GB/month, Item/month, h/month and an empty unit. isMRC is true on some records whose unit is h, so neither field alone tells an hourly rate from a monthly one. (KB-HYBW, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- The service description lists flavor x1e.slarge.2 with 3 vCPU and 6 GB RAM, a specification the flavor name does not reflect. (KB-IDKJ, contract, checked 2026-07-01)
- The Financial Dashboard has a consumption view by tag with hourly and daily granularity and key/value selection, backed by the Business.tag_daily and Business.tag_hourly tables. Tagged resource costs appear in the invoice CSV. The invoice document stays per tenant and not every product is taggable. (KB-JDBN, docs, checked 2026-07-17)
- The public price API returns money values in English number format: a comma separates thousands and a dot separates decimals, followed by the currency code. A parser that reads the comma as a decimal separator returns values a thousand times too low. (KB-NCPE, live, checked 2026-06-11)
- The price API switches to the announced value on the effective date. For the flavor families the API covers, the from-column of the service description and the API value agree to six decimals. (KB-PL7K, live, checked 2026-07-05)
- The eu-ch2 slice of the public TCP price API is not the Swiss price list: on 2026-10-08 it held 75 records marked EUR, 15 of them (KMS API calls, the five OBS Standard Space tiers, eight SMN rows) with exactly the numbers the Swiss calculator shows as CHF, and 60 rows that the Swiss calculator does not carry at all (API Gateway, HSS, WAF domain, FunctionGraph, LTS, DNS queries, OBS cold and warm space, outbound traffic, a firewall and VPN row), most of them priced zero. It carries no ECS, EVS or RDS row for eu-ch2, and its values were unchanged between 2026-09-24 and 2026-10-08. (KB-PRGT, live, checked 2026-10-08)
- The service description lists UCS rates in section 6.8.5, billed per hour and per CPU in the cluster, with the same rate for clusters inside and outside the tenant and the eu-nl region marked as not applicable. (KB-QIV4, contract, checked 2026-09-01)
- Prices of the Swiss offering (region eu-ch2) come from a price calculator of its own on the operator's Swiss site (calculator client 2), in CHF: on 2026-10-08 it had 19 modules with 255 records, among them 146 ECS rows (s3 flavors; Open Linux, Oracle Linux, RedHat, SUSE, SUSE for SAP and Windows tiers), 40 RDS rows, Direct Connect, Dedicated Host, CCE, EVS (SAS and SSD), OBS, EIP, ELB, NAT, private DNS, VPC, VPC endpoint, KMS, SMN, CBR, SDRS, AOM and dedicated WAF, with pay-as-you-use and 12-month reserved packages. The main TCP calculator lists eu-ch2 in its region picker but its module picker returns no module for that region. (KB-QWED, live, checked 2026-10-08)
- In the price API, sn=ecs covers only the s2, s3, s7n and s9 families. The vss.* entries in that payload are EVS disk SKUs rather than a compute family, and the other ECS families sit under other service names of the price API. (KB-RMW2, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- The price API returns rows and the documentation mirror keeps full user guides and API references for services that are no longer offered, VBS and CSBS (end of life since 01.01.2026) among them, so presence in either source does not show that a service is orderable. For BMS in eu-de on 2026-09-25, the legacy BMS flavors are all abandon or sellout, while the bms rows of the price API now cover the orderable Physical ECS flavor c7t.28xl. (KB-SCLG, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In the public price API the service name sn=dcaas returns HTTP 500, while sn=dc with rn=eu-de returns the full Direct Connect price list including bandwidth tiers and ports. The same service can be served under an abbreviation different from the one used elsewhere. (KB-SEDA, live, checked 2026-08-25)
- The public price API returns a changing set of ECS records: 502, then 508, then 400 within one month, with the x1 family and the s3 R36 reserved fields disappearing between calls. (KB-SFUF, live, checked 2026-07-01)
- Reserved price rows can be left out of a pre-announced increase. In the change effective 01.07.2026 the R12, R24 and R36 cells carried no from-date marker and their values did not move. (KB-SUE5, contract, checked 2026-07-05)
- The public TCP price API answers at https://calculator.otc-service.com/en/open-telekom-price-api/ with JSON and needs no key. It takes sn for the service name and rn for the region. limitFrom pages through the records and limitMax sets how many come per page: 500 without it, while limitMax=10000 returned each region in one answer on 2026-09-24. (KB-W7ZS, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- The public price API held these record counts on 2026-09-24: eu-de 3386 records under 100 service names, eu-nl 2638 records under 64 names and eu-ch2 75 records under 14 names. (KB-XEGQ, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- A record in the public price API does not show that a service can still be ordered: rows stay served for services that have reached end of life, so orderability has to be confirmed against the service catalog. (KB-XELM, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- Record ids of the price API are specific to a region: on 2026-09-24 none of the 6099 ids of eu-de, eu-nl and eu-ch2 appeared in two regions, so an id also fixes the region. (KB-XJDA, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- SKU ids seen in the price API for network and object items include OTC_OBST_SP_2 (OBS Standard storage), OTC_OBST_REQ (OBS requests per 1000), OTC_LBV3_B_2AZ_20 (dedicated ELB, 2 AZ, 20 LCU), OTC_ELASTIC_IP and OTC_VPC_UP_S_2 (egress tier). (KB-YAT6, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- In eu-de on 2026-09-24 the price API held the x1 family under sn=ecsflex in the size ratios 2, 3 and 4 only, 18 flavors, and held no m3 flavor under any of the six ECS service names. (KB-YUVN, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- A reservation is bound to the instance type it was bought for and does not follow a downsize of the instance. (KB-ZJ4B, contract, checked 2026-07-01)
- Records of the price API with idGroupTiered set carry the bounds of their tier in fromOn and upTo. On 2026-09-24 there were 355 such records in 18 groups in eu-de and eu-nl, most of them outbound internet traffic (group OTC_VPC_UP) and OBS outbound traffic. (KB-ZNJK, live, checked 2026-09-24)

## evs (38)

- Elastic Volume Service (EVS) provides block storage volumes that are attached to Elastic Cloud Servers as system or data disks; up to 32 volumes can be attached to one server and a volume can be expanded online when the operating system supports it. The disk types are High I/O, GP SSD, GP SSD V2, Ultra-High I/O and Extreme SSD, and the documentation states that system and data disks support encryption, snapshots and backups. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for EVS in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-2DY5, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- On a Linux ECS an attached EVS data disk appears as a virtio block device such as /dev/vdb. A file system is created on the device before it is mounted. (KB-3URW, live, checked 2026-05-01)
- Releasing an attached EVS volume takes two calls in two different services. Detach runs through the ECS API as DELETE /v1/{project_id}/cloudservers/{server_id}/detachvolume/{volume_id} and is asynchronous, so its job must reach SUCCESS first. Only then does DELETE /v2/{project_id}/cloudvolumes/{volume_id} on the EVS API remove the volume. (KB-4V7Z, live, checked 2026-05-01)
- EVS shared disks on T Cloud Public (TCP) support SCSI-3 reservations and can be attached to up to 16 servers. The attached ECS instances have to sit in an anti-affinity ECS group. (KB-4WQV, docs, checked 2026-08-24)
- A 430 GB AWQ-INT4 model cold-load took 50 to 180 minutes from a GPSSD volume and about 7 minutes from an ESSD volume on the same GPU instance type. (KB-56WX, live, checked 2026-05-07)
- An 80 GiB restored Windows system disk reported 0 percent lazy loading at 13 s, 6 percent at 137 s and 100 percent at 438 s after the create call, while RDP answered at about 149 s. The counter is non-monotonic near the end, only the final value is reliable. (KB-5DQ4, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- An EVS data disk attached to a Windows Server 2019 or 2022 ECS supports ReFS block cloning. Backup software reported fast cloning as supported after the volume was formatted ReFS with GPT, a 64 KB cluster size and integrity streams off. (KB-6Z6K, live, checked 2026-04-22)
- EVS encryption happens at the volume layer, so an encrypted data disk is partitioned, formatted and mounted inside a Linux guest exactly like an unencrypted one. (KB-7ZUP, live, checked 2026-06-15)
- POST /v2.1/{project_id}/cloudvolumes creates an EVS volume asynchronously and returns a job_id. That job is polled on the EVS jobs endpoint /v1/{project_id}/jobs/{job_id} rather than the ECS jobs endpoint, and the new volume id appears as entities.volume_id once the job status is SUCCESS. (KB-CZH3, live, checked 2026-05-01)
- Lazy-loading progress of a restored disk is read with GET /v3/{project_id}/os-vendor-volumes/{volume_id}/internal-info. The live response carries the field lazyloading_progress, while the documentation names the field loading_progress. (KB-DLKD, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- The TCP service description states that up to 32 block storage devices may be assigned per Elastic Cloud Server, while the ECS user manual states 24 at creation and up to 60 after attachment (VBD max 24, SCSI max 59, VBD plus SCSI at most 60). The two sources disagree and neither number is settled. (KB-DMN5, docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- A volume create request on the EVS path /v2.1/{project_id}/cloudvolumes carries availability_zone in the volume body, for example eu-de-01, and the volume is created in that AZ. (KB-FSXW, live, checked 2026-05-01)
- An EVS disk consumed in CCE through a dynamic PV is bound to one AZ and can be mounted by one pod at a time. (KB-GZTZ, docs, checked 2026-09-16)
- Data of a disk restored from CBR loads lazily from OBS after the mount. A full read took 183 seconds for 8 GiB and 1780 seconds for 81 GiB, about 22 seconds per GiB or roughly 47 MB/s, and it applies per disk. (KB-HJKW, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- On a running Linux ECS with a virtio_blk system disk (ext4 on GPT), an extended EVS system disk grows online with growpart and resize2fs, without reboot. Verified from 30 GB to 100 GB while the filesystem was mounted. (KB-HNAB, live, checked 2026-06-08)
- A root EVS volume on a running ECS can be extended online. After the cloud-side extend, growpart on the root device followed by resize2fs grows a mounted ext4 partition while the instance keeps running, verified from 30 GB to 100 GB on a virtio_blk GPT disk. (KB-IP25, live, checked 2026-06-08)
- EVS General Purpose SSD (GPSSD) behaved like a burst-credit volume similar to AWS gp2 in a test: fresh volumes burst above baseline, and after hours of heavy reads sustained reads fell to about 21 MB/s (small random reads, about 285 IOPS). That made loading large model weights disk-bound. (KB-JKEF, live, checked 2026-05-07)
- EVS GP SSD v2 bills on three separate meters on TCP: provisioned capacity, provisioned IOPS above the 3,000 IOPS included, and provisioned throughput above the 125 MiB/s included. (KB-JN3T, contract, checked 2026-09-09)
- Bringing a 10 GiB GPT data volume back on a Windows Server 2022 guest took 43 s in total: 6 s to create the volume from the CBR backup, 12 s to attach it, the rest to bring it online. The volume arrives Offline under the SAN policy OfflineShared. (KB-KQUM, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- Data disks under heavy write survive a crash-consistent capture by both SDRS mirroring and CBR backup: both copies reported Filesystem state clean, mounted, and passed e2fsck -fn. The file count differs between the copies, which is expected for a point-in-time capture. (KB-KUQ7, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A CBR backup can be restored into another availability zone: POST /v2/{project_id}/cloudvolumes on the EVS endpoint accepts backup_id together with a target availability_zone, volume_type and size, and creates the disk there. (KB-LXHJ, live, checked 2026-08-26)
- An EVS snapshot stays bound to its source volume and goes away with it, so a copy that has to outlive the disk is taken as an IMS system disk image or a CBR backup. (KB-MDYY, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- In eu-de the EVS volume listing GET /v2/{project_id}/cloudvolumes/detail returns count and volumes, and each entry carries id, name, size, volume_type, bootable and a metadata object. The metadata carries __system__encrypted on some volumes only, with value 1 or 0, and __system__cmkid only on encrypted volumes, while the top-level encrypted field read false even on encrypted volumes (checked 2026-09-25). (KB-O5MK, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The size of the volume actually attached to an ECS is read from the EVS cloudvolumes/detail response, matching attachments[].server_id against the instance UUID from the metadata service at /openstack/latest/meta_data.json. A guest still enumerating the old size after a clean boot points to a volume that was not extended. (KB-P3SX, live, checked 2026-06-08)
- On an ECS with virtio_blk disks, the guest picks up an extended EVS volume size automatically through a virtio config-change interrupt. /sys/class/block/vda/size shows the new size before growpart is run. (KB-RGHC, live, checked 2026-06-08)
- ESSD was available in eu-de-01 when checked. The volume types offered per availability zone are listed by GET https://evs.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com/v3/{project_id}/types. (KB-SOBS, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- An EVS snapshot moves from creating to available on GET /v2/{project_id}/snapshots/{snapshot_id}. For a 40 GB GPSSD root disk that took about five seconds. (KB-SY3Y, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- A virtio_blk disk picks up a new volume size through a virtio config-change interrupt once the volume was really extended. The kernel view can be read from /sys/class/block/<dev>/size or with fdisk, and it already shows the new capacity when the event arrived. (KB-T4IL, live, checked 2026-06-08)
- EVS Extreme SSD reaches at most 1,000 MiB/s and 128,000 IOPS with sub-millisecond latency, but its throughput is Min(1,000, 120 + 0.5 x capacity in GiB): the full rate, which loads a 430 GB model in about 7 minutes, needs a disk of about 1,760 GiB, while a disk sized just for the model gets about 320 MiB/s. Extreme SSD also has an IOPS burst token bucket (burst limit 64,000) and General Purpose SSD is capped at 250 MiB/s. (KB-TUFK, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Creating an EVS volume from a CBR backup takes about 6 to 7 s and barely depends on volume size, measured at 8 GiB and at 81 GiB. Volume size affects the background lazy load, not the create step. (KB-UH3H, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- POST /v2/{project_id}/snapshots on EVS needs force set to true when the source volume is attached to a server; without it the request for an in-use volume is refused. The call returns HTTP 202 with a snapshot id in status creating. (KB-UQLA, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- On a Linux guest, reading a restored volume end to end took 183 s for 8 GiB and 1780 s for 81 GiB while the background load was still running. (KB-V2AA, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- Deleting an EVS disk deletes all data of the disk including the snapshots created from it, so an EVS snapshot does not outlive its source disk as it would in plain Cinder. An EVS snapshot is not an independent copy. (KB-W5BY, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- EVS volumes are triple-replicated on the platform side, so capacity is planned at net capacity: a mirrored pair of two 3.84 TB on-premises disks corresponds to about 3.84 TB of EVS, not 7.68 TB. (KB-WKHA, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- After hours of heavy reads a GPSSD volume drained its burst credits and sustained reads fell to about 21 MB/s (iostat -x showed about 285 IOPS at roughly 10 ms r_await), against a baseline near 150 MB/s. An ESSD volume in the same test sustained about 1 GB/s with sub-millisecond latency. (KB-XXZV, live, checked 2026-05-07)
- Deleting an EVS volume also deletes the snapshots taken from it, so a snapshot lives only as long as its source disk. An IMS image or a CBR backup survives deletion of the source instance. (KB-YENF, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- An EVS data volume created from the backup child of an encrypted volume together with a destination KMS key comes up encrypted at the destination, without an OS-level copy of the data. (KB-YEPK, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The EVS path /v3/{project_id}/cloudvolumes/detail returns HTTP 404 in TCP eu-de. The same volume detail listing is served by the v2 path. (KB-YIBL, live, checked 2026-09-25)

## other (38)

- HetuEngine in MRS 3.5.0-LTS does not support registering an Iceberg catalog as shipped, because HSConsole has no ICEBERG data source type. Workaround: repoint the template of an unused type in HSConsole etc/datasource.properties, restart HetuEngine, then POST the catalog to /v1/hsconsole/datasource on port 29880. (KB-2KDP, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- The base service description, revision 17.08.2026, section 4.2, names a single availability figure: 99.95% per calendar month, covering Elastic Cloud Server, Object Storage Service and Elastic Volume Service. For all other services it states that there is no minimum availability. (KB-35HK, contract, checked 2026-09-01)
- FunctionGraph scales to zero when idle and idle time is not charged. (KB-3FHX, docs, checked 2026-08-27)
- FunctionGraph is a managed serverless service that hosts and runs event-driven functions: the customer writes code and sets the trigger conditions, and the service manages the compute resources and scales automatically with the number of requests. Functions are written in Node.js, Python, Java, C#, PHP, Go or a custom runtime and are triggered by API calls or cloud service events; reserved instances keep functions warm against cold starts. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the FunctionGraph API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-3L75, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- Managed Services are optional operations services that Telekom provides on T Cloud Public (TCP) in three packages, Managed Services Basic, Standard and Advanced, covering up to 20, 50 or 150 managed services (operating systems, databases and applications). The portfolio catalogue names applications (Nginx, Tomcat, Apache, firewall, VPN, GitLab), RDS for SQL Server, MySQL and PostgreSQL and self-run SQL Server, MySQL, Oracle, MariaDB and PostgreSQL, managed Linux and Windows Server operating systems and managed containers; larger or custom scopes are offered individually. (KB-4N6Q, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- MRS Spark defaults to spark.sql.storeAssignmentPolicy=LEGACY, which DataSource V2 rejects with 'LEGACY store assignment policy is disallowed in Spark data source V2'. Set --conf spark.sql.storeAssignmentPolicy=ANSI for any Iceberg INSERT. (KB-4Y2C, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- DORA Article 28(8) requires financial entities to put in place exit strategies for ICT services supporting critical or important functions, with comprehensive and documented exit plans that are tested and reviewed periodically, and with transition plans to move the services and data to another provider or back in-house. (KB-6YSY, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Enterprise Agreement 1.0 (EA10) is an optional extended service contract from Telekom in the service classes Silver, Gold and Platinum, ordered separately on top of the standard service. All classes add a consulting and administration service; Gold and Platinum add IT architecture consulting, an availability report and service meetings; Platinum adds dedicated service and delivery management and a manager on duty service. Service levels, response times and credits are contract terms and are not stated here. (KB-7U4I, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- Data Admin Service (DAS) is a cloud database management platform in preview for managing databases on a web console, with database development, operations and intelligent diagnosis. The service description of 17.08.2026 announces it as expected to be available from 1 May 2027, and as a preview version it is meant for testing. (KB-AB67, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- Secure Mail Gateway (SMG) lets applications hosted on T Cloud Public (TCP) send email to the internet through a gateway service, so the sending servers' own IP addresses do not land on spam blacklists; outbound SMTP on ports 465 and 587 is opened only towards this gateway. The documentation names anti-virus and anti-spam checks, three mail servers spread over eu-de-01, eu-de-02 and eu-de-03, and a possible limit of 100 mails per minute per customer, which the service description also reserves. (KB-BGAJ, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- FunctionGraph runs in eu-de only: on 2026-09-25 GET /v2/{project_id}/fgs/functions answered HTTP 200 on functiongraph.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com and HTTP 404 APIGW.0101 on the eu-nl endpoint. (KB-BGBR, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Consulting Services (CS) are optional consulting services from Telekom in the T Cloud Public (TCP) environment, commissioned separately through the T Cloud Public Support Center, that go beyond the support services of the standard contract. (KB-CXIW, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- The public platform is branded T Cloud Public (TCP) after the 2025 rename. The service description carries the title "Service Specifications & Additional Terms and Conditions T Cloud Public". (KB-DFXG, contract, checked 2026-07-15)
- On the shared default DLI queue, statements stayed in LAUNCHING for over 15 minutes and the synchronous submit returned DLI.0019 Get sync job result timeout; the job id is in the error text. Capability tests need a dedicated queue or elastic resource pool. (KB-ETTV, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- Section 7.3 of the TCP service description, revision of 2026-08-17, freezes a tenant for the two weeks before its termination takes effect and erases all its resources when it does, and section 7.5 requires the customer to back up its application data on its own responsibility before that grace period. (KB-F3A4, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- In a HetuEngine Iceberg data source on MRS, hive.config.resources needs absolute paths; relative paths make reads fail with 'EndPoint is not set'. (KB-FAPL, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- Marketplace is an optional service of T Cloud Public (TCP): customers subscribe to and use services from third-party providers and Telekom units through it, and sellers publish software offers through the Marketplace Seller Center, on request. A product offering combines the service type, which is Cloud Container Engine, the license type (open source, free or trial, bring your own license, or private offer) and the seller's software. (KB-FFRB, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- Uploading core-site.xml as a file-typed property of a HetuEngine data source in MRS corrupts the file, and reads then fail with WstxIOException: Invalid UTF-8 start byte 0x81. (KB-FLDL, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- Tag Management Service (TMS) adds key-value tags as metadata to cloud resources and manages them centrally across services and regions; tags are created by hand or predefined and imported in CSV format, and resources are searched and filtered by tag. Costs can be assigned to tags in the invoice documents and in the Financial Dashboard. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for TMS in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-HFN2, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- DORA Article 30(3)(f) makes exit strategies a mandatory term of contracts for ICT services supporting critical or important functions, in particular an adequate transition period in which the ICT third-party service provider keeps providing the service while the financial entity moves to another provider or in-house. Article 30(2)(d) requires every ICT services contract to provide for access, recovery and return of the data on termination or on the provider's insolvency, resolution or end of business. (KB-K5U4, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In MRS 3.5.0-LTS, impalad runs on the core nodes and listens on ports 21050 and 21000, so Impala clients connect to a core node. (KB-K6OT, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- Config (Resource Management Service, RMS) lets the customer centrally view all resources of a tenant with their details and relationships. The documentation adds a resource recorder that keeps change records and snapshots in an OBS bucket and sends change notifications through Simple Message Notification, compliance evaluation of resources against Config rules, and advanced queries in ResourceQL. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for Config in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-KHDV, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- Section 7.2.3 of the TCP service description, revision of 2026-08-17, gives a customer who notifies a migration under the Data Act at least 30 days ahead a 30-day transition period. The customer may extend it once, and it never exceeds seven months. (KB-KT7I, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- Announced availability dates in the service description move between revisions. UCS was 01.04.2026 in rev. 08.06.2026 and 01.09.2026 in rev. 17.08.2026, and CSMS moved from 01.07.2026 to 01.10.2026 and then to 01.04.2027. (KB-LQBE, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- Data Replication Service (DRS) migrates and synchronizes databases online in real time, to simplify data migration and reduce migration effort; the customer gets tools to query the migration progress, check migration logs and compare migrated items. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for DRS in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-LSZV, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- API Gateway (dedicated) (APIG) is a managed API hosting service whose gateways run dedicated to the customer's tenant; it covers the whole API lifecycle of creating, debugging, publishing and taking APIs offline. Request throttling, authentication, environments with environment variables, signatures, version rollback and API monitoring with alarms are configured per API. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the APIG API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-MIJ4, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- Base support in the service description answers within 4 hours on a best-effort basis. Critical cases are handled 24/7, all other cases Monday to Friday from 8 to 17. (KB-MM3J, contract, checked 2026-09-16)
- DORA Article 2(2) calls only the entities of points (a) to (t) of Article 2(1) financial entities, so ICT third-party service providers, listed separately under point (u), are not the addressees of the exit duty in Article 28, and Article 28(1)(a) keeps financial entities fully responsible for compliance when they use ICT services. (KB-NLXT, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- hetu-cli creates a HetuEngine compute instance in MRS without the web UI. The user needs a Manager role with Submit and Admin on YARN queue root.default (without it hetu-cli reports 'user does not belong to the specified tenant') and membership in the hetuuser group, which takes effect only after a HetuEngine restart. (KB-NVXS, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- Enterprise Support Agreement 2.0 (ESA20) is an optional extended support package from Telekom, ordered separately. The basic package includes the Financial Dashboard and a consulting and administration service; dedicated service delivery management and a service delivery manager on duty are optional components. Service levels, response times and credits are contract terms and are not stated here. (KB-NZSQ, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
