- Optical Character Recognition (OCR) automatically recognizes printed and handwritten characters in images and converts them into editable text. It is used through open APIs, and the documentation describes general OCR that extracts text from images of tables, documents, receipts and forms and returns it in JSON format. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the OCR API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-OBHQ, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- Financial Dashboard (FD, former Enterprise Dashboard) gives the customer an overview of the resources used and their costs in a separate portal reached through MyWorkplace, with predefined and custom diagrams, CSV and API export, and email notification when customer-set cost thresholds are reached. It comes as Small, which is included in the standard scope, Medium and Large; Medium and Large add support for several T Cloud Public tenants and Large adds organization management. (KB-QAFU, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- The DORA oversight framework of Chapter V Section II applies to ICT third-party service providers that the ESAs designate as critical under Article 31(1)(a), and under Article 31(9) the ESAs publish and update the list of critical providers yearly. (KB-QILM, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Oracle-optimized (OO) instances are virtual servers with an Oracle-optimized operating system and ZFS storage classes built for Oracle databases, offered in two availability zones of the EU-DE region. The service supports Oracle Enterprise Edition with the customer's own license (BYOL) only, needs a Direct Connect connection and an initial network setup in the customer tenant, and high availability is built by the customer with instances in different availability zones and database replication the customer sets up. (KB-U7RP, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- Database Security Service (DBSS) audits databases: it records user access to the database in real time, detects SQL injection attacks and high-risk operations, sends real-time alerts and produces audit and compliance reports. The documentation describes out-of-path audit of RDS databases and of databases the customer runs on Elastic Cloud Servers, among them TaurusDB, MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQL Server. DBSS is offered in eu-de (KB-22YR, KB-JM53). (KB-UUZF, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- Section 7.2.4 of the TCP service description, revision of 2026-08-17, points to the documentation and a switching information page to support the customer's exit strategy, and section 7.2.5 makes the customer responsible for performing the migration, exporting its content and choosing and configuring the new provider, with no liability of the operator for the success of the migration. (KB-VNL6, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- Distributed Database Middleware (DDM) is a MySQL-compatible distributed middleware service for relational databases that removes capacity and performance limits of Relational Database Service (RDS) instances for large data volumes at high access rates. The documentation names automatic database and table sharding across several data nodes, read/write splitting and scaling of compute nodes and of shards. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the DDM API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-WVTD, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- Alert Service is an optional preview service that tracks the customer's spending on T Cloud Public (TCP) and is reached through MyWorkplace. The service description of 17.08.2026 announced it as expected to be available from 1 October 2026, and as a preview version it is meant for testing. (KB-XKXQ, contract, checked 2026-10-11)

## rds-mssql (38)

- RDS for SQL Server supports instances joined to an AD domain, and the documentation marks that setup as not recommended. (KB-4SGC, docs, checked 2026-08-25)
- In a DRS backup migration the database name is matched case-sensitively against the name stored inside the backup file. The names master, model, msdb, tempdb, rdsadmin and resource are refused as a source name and as a new name. (KB-5VMM, docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- A create call for RDS for SQL Server takes datastore type SQLServer with a version string such as 2017_SE, 2019_SE, 2022_SE, 2017_EE, 2019_EE or 2022_EE, and a matching flavor_ref, for example rds.mssql.x1.large.2 for a single Standard Edition instance. (KB-5VPL, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- SQL Server on TCP runs through managed RDS for SQL Server with the licence included, through the customer's own licence on a Dedicated Host or Physical ECS (service description section 3.1.4.2), or through Managed Services, whose eu-de price list carries Custom MSSQL with Standard and Enterprise licence rows as of 2026-09-25. (KB-752O, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- In the RDS for SQL Server create call the collation is validated before the KMS key: a request with a bogus disk_encryption_id UUID returns DBS.200506 when the collation is valid and DBS.200005 when it is not, and the request is rejected before an instance is created. (KB-7IPK, live, checked 2026-05-22)
- Backup and restore of an RDS for SQL Server instance through SSMS is not available. Backup runs through the managed service. (KB-ATOA, docs, checked 2026-08-25)
- Backup of an RDS for SQL Server instance on T Cloud Public (TCP) fails once the instance holds more than 500,000 tables. (KB-B54W, docs, checked 2026-08-24)
- RDS for SQL Server on TCP offers engine versions up to SQL Server 2022. (KB-BCY3, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- DRS backup migration expects the destination engine version to match the source or be newer. An Enterprise Edition source can fail against a Standard Edition destination, depending on which Enterprise-only features the source database used. (KB-BETZ, docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- RDS for SQL Server grants no super administrator (sysadmin) permission to any user, and the documentation directs applications to connect with a lower role. (KB-DQ7I, docs, checked 2026-08-25)
- A primary/standby switchover on the destination RDS instance during a DRS backup migration fails the task permanently, and the task has to be created again from the start. (KB-FP7M, docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- In the RDS price listing the license and storage rows carry an empty opiFlavour, so they are selected by osUnit, which holds the edition such as Standard or Enterprise, and by productCategory, which is MSSQL for SQL Server rows. (KB-IVQL, live, checked 2026-06-01)
- On 2026-07-06 the RDS datastore listing for sqlserver in eu-de returned six versions: 2017_SE, 2017_EE, 2019_SE, 2019_EE, 2022_SE and 2022_EE. The newest edition in that listing was 2022. (KB-JYO7, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Pre-verification in a DRS backup migration is on by default. It reads the backup file header from OBS and checks validity, integrity, continuity and engine version compatibility before the restore starts, taking one to two minutes. (KB-LE6N, docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- On eu-de the DRS backup migration task type is driven from the console only; the public DRS API exposes no backup_migration job type. (KB-MEQU, live, checked 2026-05-20)
- The RDS for SQL Server create call validates fields in a fixed order and stops at the first error: availability zone DBS.280244, flavor_ref DBS.280434, port DBS.280364, volume size DBS.280266, name DBS.280234, password DBS.280203, collation DBS.200005, then disk_encryption_id DBS.200506. (KB-MGFM, live, checked 2026-05-22)
- The volume size of an RDS for SQL Server instance has to be a multiple of 10 GB; another value fails the create call with DBS.280266. (KB-NEKR, live, checked 2026-05-22)
- Overwrite Data set to Yes in a DRS backup migration disables HA on the destination RDS instance for the duration of the task and restores HA when the task ends. (KB-NGJR, docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- A source database with TDE enabled has to have database-level TDE turned off before DRS backup migration accepts it. (KB-NKEB, docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- Latin1_General_100_CI_AS is accepted by the RDS for SQL Server create call although the /collations listing endpoint leaves it out of its result. (KB-OI2G, live, checked 2026-05-22)
- After a DRS backup migration the restored .mdf occupies its allocated size, not the used size. One restore produced a 163 GB file from 73 GB of user data, so destination storage has to cover the allocated size, around 1.5 times the backup database size. (KB-P5SX, live, checked 2026-05-20)
- A native SQL Server backup file restored by DRS carries database-level content only. Logins, server permissions, linked servers, SQL Agent jobs and DB Mail stay on the source side, and non-clustered indexes arrive in an invalid state. (KB-PRHV, docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- On 2026-08-24 RDS for SQL Server in eu-de offered the versions 2017, 2019 and 2022, each in Standard and Enterprise edition. (KB-QLJ5, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In the RDS for SQL Server create call the storage type is validated before the collation. A flavour whose AZ status in eu-de-01 is other than normal returns DBS.280241 Invalid storage type and reports nothing about the collation. (KB-QPF3, live, checked 2026-08-24)
- The api-ref for the SQL Server collation query gives the URI as /v3/{project_id}/collations while its own example shows /v3/{project_id}/instances/{instance_id}/collations, and its sample response values are generic filler rather than the eu-de list. (KB-RKCX, docs, checked 2026-08-24)
- The RDS SQL Server collation list is read with GET /v3/{project_id}/collations, a project-scoped path with no instance id, and the values arrive under the response key charSets. (KB-RSU3, live, checked 2026-08-24)
- RDS for SQL Server offers engine versions 2017, 2019 and 2022, each in Standard and Enterprise edition. SQL Server 2025 (engine 17.00) is not offered in the API, the console or the service description. (KB-RUHF, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Documented RDS for SQL Server limits: database names up to 64 characters from [a-zA-Z0-9_-], at most 100 databases per instance, and backup fails above 500000 tables. (KB-RYBN, docs, checked 2026-08-25)
- RDS for SQL Server accepts eight collations at create time: Chinese_PRC_CI_AS, Chinese_PRC_CI_AI, Chinese_PRC_90_CI_AI, SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS, SQL_Latin1_General_CP850_BIN2, Cyrillic_General_CI_AS, Latin1_General_100_CI_AS_KS and Latin1_General_100_CI_AS. Any other value, including legacy Latin1_General_* variants without _100_ and other European locales, returns DBS.200005 Server failure. (KB-SCS5, live, checked 2026-05-22)
- DRS backup migration reads the .bak from an OBS bucket in the same region as the target RDS instance and only from the bucket root. The file picker lists .bak files only, and the file name is limited to 200 characters of letters, digits, hyphen and underscore. (KB-SDB5, docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- SQL Server flavour keys in the RDS listing follow the form db.x1.<size>.<ratio>.<mode>.mssql, where mode is single, ha or rr. In that listing the hourly rate of an ha flavour was exactly twice the rate of the matching single flavour. (KB-SVWO, live, checked 2026-06-01)
- RDS for SQL Server on T Cloud Public (TCP) allows a maximum of 100 databases per instance. (KB-TQJT, docs, checked 2026-08-24)
- A DRS Backup Migration restore onto RDS for SQL Server keeps the collation of the source user database. In a 162 GiB restore the database arrived as Latin1_General_CI_AS on an instance whose server collation was Latin1_General_100_CI_AS_KS. (KB-TTWD, live, checked 2026-05-01)
- RDS for SQL Server in eu-de offers Latin1_General_CI_AS as an instance collation: on 2026-08-24 it was both in the collation listing and accepted by the create call. (KB-UGZC, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- DRS backup migration requires each backup file to hold exactly one database. A database whose backup is spread over several files is outside what the task accepts. (KB-W72N, docs, checked 2026-05-20)
- On 2026-08-24 the collation listing in eu-de returned 8 values: Chinese_PRC_CI_AS, Chinese_PRC_CI_AI, Chinese_PRC_90_CI_AI, Cyrillic_General_CI_AS, SQL_Latin1_General_CP1_CI_AS, SQL_Latin1_General_CP850_BIN2, Latin1_General_100_CI_AS_KS and Latin1_General_CI_AS. The same listing returned 7 on 2026-05-22, with Latin1_General_CI_AS added since. (KB-WESI, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On RDS for SQL Server the server collation is fixed at instance creation, so tempdb, model, msdb and the system catalogs keep it while a restored user database keeps its own. Statements crossing the two, such as a join to a #temp table, fail with Msg 468. (KB-YF25, live, checked 2026-05-01)
- On 2026-09-25 the eu-de storage-type listing for RDS for SQL Server offered CLOUDSSD, ESSD and GPSSD2 for Enterprise and Standard versions alike and marked ULTRAHIGH unsupported in every AZ. The create API documents CLOUDSSD, ESSD and GPSSD2 as volume types. (KB-ZHA4, live, checked 2026-09-25)

## cts (36)

- PUT /v3/{project_id}/tracker refuses compress_type in upper case with error CTS.0227 'The compress_type must be json or gzip'; the lower-case value gzip is accepted. (KB-2QGH, live, checked 2026-09-23)
- CTS in eu-de records public NAT gateway operations under service_type VPC (deleteSnatRule, deleteNatGateway, updateFloatingip, deleteEip), not under NAT: a query with service_type=NAT for the same window returned no traces. (KB-3XKX, live, checked 2026-10-06)
- CTS in eu-de does not record an ELB API call that the service rejects: a DELETE of a listener answered 409 ELB.8907 left no trace, while the successful deletes around it did. A gap in the trail can therefore hide a failed call; the client output is the evidence for errors. (KB-4QFE, live, checked 2026-10-06)
- CTS writes transferred trace files under CloudTraces/{region}/{yyyy}/{mm}/{dd}/system/{SERVICE}/ when is_sort_by_service is true. The file_prefix_name is not a folder: it starts the file name, as in {prefix}_CloudTrace_{region}_{timestamp}Z_{hash}. (KB-5HUW, live, checked 2026-09-23)
- CTS is a control-plane audit log covering tenant API calls, console actions and IAM events. A window with no traces therefore bounds an incident to the guest operating system. (KB-7FCF, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- GET /v3/{project_id}/traces on the CTS endpoint in eu-de with trace_type=system, service_type, from and to in epoch milliseconds and limit=100 returns per trace the trace_name, resource_type, resource_name, HTTP code and trace_rating. That is enough to rebuild the order and outcome of a terraform apply or destroy, call by call, after the fact. (KB-A5RP, live, checked 2026-10-06)
- The CTS trace query API GET /v3/{project_id}/traces serves about seven days of history. limit maxes out at 200 per page and meta_data.marker is passed back as next to page further into the past. (KB-AB5E, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- CTS system traces are read with GET /v3/{project_id}/traces, using trace_type=system, limit, from and to as epoch milliseconds, and resource_id to filter a single resource. (KB-AK3I, live, checked 2026-07-31)
- An OBS setBucketPolicy call became visible in the CTS trace query about two to three minutes after it happened: the query returned nothing at first and the trace after a little over two minutes of polling every 30 seconds. (KB-FYVH, live, checked 2026-09-23)
- Cloud Trace Service keeps traces queryable through its API for 7 days in eu-de. An event older than that returns no trace, and is readable later only if an authorized OBS transfer or an enabled LTS forwarding was already active when the event happened. (KB-GJKP, live, checked 2026-07-31)
- A CTS event notification with operation_type customized requires an explicit operations list naming service_type, resource_type and trace_names, and then publishes matching traces to an SMN topic. (KB-GWL5, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- Cloud Trace Service (CTS) is a log audit service that records the operations performed on cloud resources through the console, through API calls and by connected services, for security analysis, change tracking, compliance audit and fault finding. Traces of the last seven days are searched in the console, all traces are stored in Object Storage Service and can be downloaded as a report, and the documentation adds transfer to Log Tank Service and key-event notification through Simple Message Notification. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the CTS API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-HWFP, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- CTS traces record control-plane API actions. In a review after a suspected guest compromise the CTS record was complete for API actions and clean, while what happened inside the guest operating system was not answerable from it. (KB-JBYB, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- CTS writes hash-chained digest files only for a tracker that has OBS transfer configured together with the opt-in trace-file verification switch. The digest signature is SHA256withRSA and the file hashes are MD5. (KB-KVR7, docs, checked 2026-09-01)
- GET /v3/{project_id}/traces?trace_type=system on the CTS endpoint returns the operation vocabulary of a service, including operations that have no published API path. (KB-LYUM, live, checked 2026-09-10)
- CTS traces can be filtered with trace_type=system plus a from and to millisecond window, and narrowed by resource_id for one resource or by service_type, for example IAM to read the full identity trail of new users, credentials, agency grants and logins. (KB-M4UN, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) keeps audit logs of management actions in Cloud Trace Service (CTS): operations on the resources of the services connected to CTS, made through the console or the API, are recorded as traces, traces of the last seven days are searched in the console, and a tracker transfers traces to an OBS bucket or an LTS log stream for long-term keeping (KB-ZICC). The 7-day query window was confirmed live in eu-de (KB-GJKP); coverage differs per service, for example SDRS wrote no traces in a live test (KB-HBF5). (KB-MF3H, docs, checked 2026-10-11)
- A customized CTS key event notification on OBS setBucketPolicy with the advanced filter resource_name = {bucket} delivered a mail through its SMN topic to a confirmed email subscription when that bucket's policy was written. No access policy on the SMN topic was needed for CTS in the same account. (KB-MQNL, live, checked 2026-09-23)
- The mail of a CTS key event notification states times as GMT+0100 even in German summer time: a trace at 15:14:22 UTC was shown as 16:14:22 GMT+0100. The mail carries the full trace, including the user name, the account and project ids and the source IP. (KB-MV5W, live, checked 2026-09-23)
- GET /v3/{project_id}/traces?trace_type=system&service_type=VPC on cts.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com returns the system traces for EIP work. The traces createEip, batchUpdateEipTags and deleteEip appeared about 20 seconds after the corresponding API call. (KB-NPWT, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- A CTS trace query filters by service_type only, so record counts over a time window measure all tenant activity rather than one procedure. In one window, 61 of 167 IMS records were queryImage. (KB-NY2U, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- GET /v3/{project_id}/trackers on the CTS endpoint answers HTTP 200. A tracker carries lts.is_lts_enabled, obs_info for transfer of traces to an object storage bucket and is_support_validate for trace file integrity, and these three are off by default. (KB-O4MB, live, checked 2026-07-02)
- A CTS trace routed to SMN and then to FunctionGraph released a non-compliant EIP about four seconds after its creation in eu-de. (KB-O6VO, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- POST /v3/{project_id}/notifications refuses a notification_name with hyphens with error cts.0007 'Notification name verify failed' and accepts underscores. A customized notification with an SMN topic and an advanced filter such as resource_name = {name} or resource_id = {id} with condition AND is created with status enabled and type smn. (KB-PHG6, live, checked 2026-09-23)
- Setting is_obs_created true in PUT /v3/{project_id}/tracker did not create a bucket or change obs_info in eu-de, although the call answered 200. (KB-R5XP, live, checked 2026-09-23)
- A CTS trace query with resource_type=security-group-rule returned zero traces for security group rule changes made minutes earlier, while older time windows in the same tenant returned complete results. (KB-RMYI, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- In a chain of CTS event notification to SMN to FunctionGraph, the remediating API call was recorded in CTS about four seconds after the event that triggered it. (KB-SLEN, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- With an existing bucket in the same account and is_obs_created false, PUT /v3/{project_id}/tracker switches the trace transfer to that bucket. The tracker then shows status enabled and is_authorized_bucket false, yet trace files arrived in the bucket within about a minute with no bucket policy set. (KB-TCSN, live, checked 2026-09-23)
- GET /v3/{project_id}/trackers returns the system tracker with status, lts.is_lts_enabled and obs_info fields including is_authorized_bucket and is_obs_created. Those fields show whether trace archiving to OBS or LTS is really active. (KB-TU5O, live, checked 2026-07-31)
- CTS records OBS bucket-policy changes as setBucketPolicy and deleteBucketPolicy with resource_type bucket and the bucket name as resource_name. IAM createCredential traces carry the new access key id as resource_id and do not name the user; updateUser and login traces carry the target user's id as resource_id. (KB-UWP4, live, checked 2026-09-23)
- CTS shows platform-internal actors beside the tenant's own identities: an op_service account materializes resources started from the console, and any agency created in the tenant appears as an actor too. Both are normal traffic in the audit trail. (KB-V232, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- In TCP eu-de on 2026-09-25 the documented CTS tracker queries GET /v3/{project_id}/trackers and GET /v1.0/{project_id}/tracker return the tracker and the trace listing GET /v3/{project_id}/traces responds normally. A GET on the singular path /v3/{project_id}/tracker, which the API reference defines only for POST and PUT, answers HTTP 404. (KB-VKNI, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- CTS recorded a manual switchover path across services: in a seven hour window CBR wrote 19 traces, IMS 167, ECS 129 and EVS at least 200, which is the page limit rather than a count. Each record holds actor, domain, source IP, time, resource and code. (KB-W44W, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A CTS trace carries user.name, source_ip, trace_name for the action (createServer, deleteEip, createCredential), trace_rating with values normal, warning or incident, trace_type with values ApiCall, ConsoleAction or SystemAction, and code. (KB-YVOM, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- CTS holds a 7 day trace window for a control plane review. Traces can be read tenant wide, filtered by resource_id for one resource, or by service_type, for example IAM, to see new users, credentials and agency grants. (KB-YXTL, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- A CTS tracker can deliver traces to an OBS bucket and to an LTS log stream. (KB-ZICC, docs, checked 2026-09-25)

## terraform (36)

- OpenTofu 1.10.3 stores remote state on OBS through the S3 backend with the skip_ flags set. init, apply and state pull complete, and the stored state objects are byte-exact across repeated applies. (KB-23WT, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- With the opentelekomcloud Terraform provider, an opentelekomcloud_networking_secgroup_v2 resource applies in about one second and destroys in about eight seconds. (KB-2NKA, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- On a test tenant in eu-de a stale credential in terraform.tfvars took effect over the same variable supplied as a TF_VAR_ environment variable. Terraform variable precedence is -var, then *.auto.tfvars, then terraform.tfvars, then TF_VAR_, then the declared default. (KB-2NR4, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- A Terraform `count` expression that resolves to a resource attribute fails with `Invalid count argument: The "count" value depends on resource attributes that cannot be determined until apply`. The same expression is evaluated during `terraform destroy`, so the destroy is blocked as well. Counts derived from input variables are known at plan time. (KB-33BW, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- The opentelekomcloud Terraform provider 1.37.1 is licensed MPL-2.0, and hashicorp/null 3.3.0 is MPL-2.0 as well. The BUSL 1.1 licence covers the Terraform engine from version 1.6.0 onward, not the providers, with a change date four years after publication. (KB-4SPW, docs, checked 2026-09-09)
- Cloud Create (former Cloud Topology Designer) is an optional design tool that lets the customer design and deploy T Cloud Public (TCP) infrastructure and the applications on it; it stores designs and configuration data in T Cloud Public and is reached at designer.otc-service.com. (KB-75AL, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- Terraform reserves count, for_each, depends_on, source, version, providers, lifecycle and provider as module meta-arguments. A module variable with one of those names fails during terraform init while terraform validate passes. The resource opentelekomcloud_asm_service_mesh_v1 carries an attribute named version, so a schema-driven generator hits this. (KB-A5BQ, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- The Terraform provider opentelekomcloud 1.37.9 authenticates in eu-de with AK/SK from OS_ACCESS_KEY and OS_SECRET_KEY, the project name as tenant_name and the account id in OS_DOMAIN_ID, without the account name: with those set, terraform plan got past the provider login, while a wrong access key ends in 'Incorrect IAM authentication information: ak ... not exist' (APIGW.0301). (KB-AIBN, live, checked 2026-10-06)
- After the rename to T Cloud Public the Terraform provider stays opentelekomcloud/opentelekomcloud, the API hosts stay under otc.t-systems.com and the docs stay on docs.otc.t-systems.com with sources in the opentelekomcloud-docs GitHub organisation, so existing code and identifiers stay unchanged. The docs portal pages now say T Cloud Public, while many service guides still say Open Telekom Cloud (docs mirror, checked 2026-09-25). (KB-BUGT, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The S3 backend option use_lockfile gives no mutual exclusion on OBS. A second apply started inside the first apply's lock window acquired the lock and exited 0, and the first apply then failed to release it. (KB-BZTT, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- The opentelekomcloud Terraform provider covers the resources around an enterprise switch build: VPC, subnet, ECS, VPC peering and the Enterprise Edition VPN stack through opentelekomcloud_enterprise_vpn_gateway_v5, opentelekomcloud_enterprise_vpn_connection_v5 and opentelekomcloud_enterprise_vpn_customer_gateway_v5. (KB-E3R7, docs, checked 2026-08-26)
- A block whose provider schema sets min_items >= 1 fails terraform validate with "Insufficient <block> blocks" when it is emitted as a dynamic block whose for_each reads an unset variable, because the count is unknown at validate time. This covers nested required blocks such as node_config then volume in opentelekomcloud_css_cluster_v1. (KB-EOI4, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- In provider opentelekomcloud the resource opentelekomcloud_ecs_instance_v1 accepts power_state, so keeping a standby instance stopped and starting it is an attribute change in the configuration rather than an imperative call. (KB-G44E, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- The opentelekomcloud provider exposes enterprise_project_id as a provider-level attribute in schema version 1.36, so the enterprise project can be set once per stack. It still takes effect only on the roughly 16 resources that are enterprise-project aware. (KB-GVDH, docs, checked 2026-07-17)
- The provider-level attributes of the opentelekomcloud Terraform provider v1.36, as listed by terraform providers schema -json, lack a default_tags block. Tags have to be set per resource, for example through a shared tagging module or variable checked by a policy test on the plan. (KB-HY3K, docs, checked 2026-07-17)
- OpenTofu 1.10.3 can keep remote state on OBS through the S3 backend with endpoint https://obs.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com and the backend's skip_* validation flags set. init, apply and state pull work and the state objects are stored byte-exact. (KB-IOPW, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- The opentelekomcloud Terraform provider reads credentials from the environment variables OS_ACCESS_KEY, OS_SECRET_KEY, OS_DOMAIN_NAME, OS_TENANT_NAME and OS_AUTH_URL, so a module whose provider block declares its own variables and one that relies on the environment can run from the same shell. (KB-J2TD, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- In provider opentelekomcloud the resource opentelekomcloud_ims_image_v2 rejects the argument backup_id with "Error: Unsupported argument". It builds an image from instance_id or volume_id, that is from a running instance or an existing volume. (KB-JM5L, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- In a live test with skip_s3_checksum unset, the OpenTofu 1.10.3 S3 backend wrote state to OBS intact: Content-Length matched the body and the object held no aws-chunked or x-amz-checksum framing. Keep skip_s3_checksum = true against future client changes. (KB-JNE2, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- In provider opentelekomcloud the resource opentelekomcloud_evs_volume_v3 accepts backup_id together with availability_zone, so a volume in another AZ can be created from a CBR backup by the resource itself with no separate API call. Checked with terraform validate against the schema, not with an apply. (KB-JQR6, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- An auto-loaded terraform.tfvars overrides a TF_VAR_ environment variable of the same name, so a credential left in a tfvars file keeps being used after the environment export is changed. (KB-L375, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- The opentelekomcloud Terraform provider authenticates with an access key and secret key together with domain_name, tenant_name and auth_url. (KB-L7RI, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In the opentelekomcloud Terraform provider, enterprise projects are set through the enterprise_project_id argument on about 16 resource types, for example opentelekomcloud_vpc_secgroup_v3, where the argument is ForceNew. (KB-LVIQ, live, checked 2026-06-03)
- Terraform provider opentelekomcloud version 1.37.7, published 2026-08-27, exposes 331 resources. (KB-LYTV, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- The schema of the Terraform provider opentelekomcloud 1.37.9 still lists five resource types of VBS and CSBS (opentelekomcloud_vbs_backup_v2, _vbs_backup_policy_v2, _vbs_backup_share_v2, _csbs_backup_v1, _csbs_backup_policy_v1), although both services are no longer available since 01.01.2026. A resource type in the provider schema does not make a service offered. (KB-M6LM, live, checked 2026-10-12)
- Provider opentelekomcloud covers cbr_vault_v3 and cbr_policy_v3, compute_volume_attach_v2 and the SDRS set sdrs_protectiongroup_v1, sdrs_protected_instance_v1, sdrs_replication_pair_v1 and sdrs_replication_attach_v1. In cbr_policy_v3 consistent_level defaults to crash_consistent, which matches what the CBR API returns. (KB-PGPD, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- Terraform's indented heredoc <<- strips the smallest indentation found on any non-empty line of the body. A nested heredoc whose interpolation and terminator lines sit at column 0 sets that baseline to 0, so the whole body keeps its indentation and the shebang arrives indented. (KB-PQ4E, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- State written to OBS by OpenTofu 1.10.3 without skip_s3_checksum carries no aws-chunked or x-amz-checksum framing: Content-Length equals the body and the object reads back as valid JSON. (KB-R5TS, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- The opentelekomcloud Terraform provider exposes enterprise projects through the enterprise_project_id argument on about 16 resources, among them vpc_secgroup_v3, lts_group_v2, fgs_function_v2, ces_*, private_nat_*, taurusdb_*, apigw_gateway_v2, cfw_firewall_v1 and waf_cloud_instance_v1. (KB-RE2U, docs, checked 2026-06-02)
- The Terraform resource opentelekomcloud_ecs_instance_v1 resizes in place on a flavor change, stop, resize and start, with no rebuild, and it ignores the image cond:image tag like the raw API does. Verified from s7n.large.4 to s9.large.2 on an untagged image. (KB-T2RV, live, checked 2026-07-21)
- On 2026-10-05 the upstream Terraform issue 36704 (skip_s3_checksum not applied to GetObject, a breaking change since Terraform 1.11.2 for S3-compatible state backends) was still open. A remote state in OBS therefore still needs Terraform pinned to 1.11.1 or OpenTofu 1.10 or newer with the skip flags and use_lockfile. (KB-TN3S, docs, checked 2026-10-05)
- The opentelekomcloud Terraform provider (schema v1.36) accepts enterprise_project_id at provider level, so one enterprise project can be set once per stack instead of on every resource. It only takes effect on resources that support enterprise projects. (KB-U52F, docs, checked 2026-07-17)
- Resource Formation Service (RFS) is a managed infrastructure-as-code engine that builds and manages cloud resources such as databases, VPCs, pipelines and IAM roles from templates, in batches and in a consistent way. The documentation states that RFS fully supports Terraform, both the HCL language and providers, and deploys the resources a template specifies automatically. (KB-UJSI, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) supports infrastructure as code: Resource Formation Service (RFS) runs Terraform templates (HCL and providers) as a managed service, the opentelekomcloud Terraform provider (version 1.37.7 exposes 331 resources, KB-LYTV) drives the platform from customer pipelines, and resources are provisioned and managed fully automatically through the platform APIs, the OpenStack APIs and standard OpenStack command-line tools. (KB-V42N, docs, checked 2026-10-11)
- With OpenTofu 1.10.3, use_lockfile = true on an OBS S3 backend gave no mutual exclusion in a live test: a second concurrent apply took the lock and overwrote the .tflock file. Serialize applies in the pipeline or use a backend with real locking. (KB-VSJN, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- The current line of the Terraform provider opentelekomcloud/opentelekomcloud for TCP is 1.37, out since 2026-07-03; the newest version on 2026-09-25 is 1.37.9. (KB-XRV5, docs, checked 2026-09-25)

## api (34)

- The enterprise project quota is 100 per account, readable with GET /quotas on the EPS endpoint. (KB-263E, live, checked 2026-06-03)
- Creating a DLI elastic resource pool via API worked with max_cu equal to min_cu: 16/16 returned 200, 16/64 returned DLI.0035. The pool took about 10 minutes to reach AVAILABLE. (KB-3VTJ, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- A TMS call to /v1.0/predefined-tags signed with AK/SK answered HTTP 404. TMS is a global service, and the call was not repeated with a domain scoped token, so the required auth type stays unconfirmed. (KB-47NM, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- Config (RMS) answers on rms.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com. GET /v1/resource-manager/domains/{domain_id}/all-resources returns a domain-wide inventory including tags and the enterprise project id, and /all-resources/summary returns counts per provider, type and region. Both need a domain-scoped token. (KB-535D, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- The canonical request ends with the SHA-256 hex digest of the request body. A request sent without a body uses the digest of the empty string in that position. (KB-66EU, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In a list query, limit=200 caps the page size rather than the number of matching records, so a full page of 200 means at least 200 records exist. (KB-6G5Y, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- An existing resource is moved into an enterprise project with POST /v1.0/enterprise-projects/{id}/resources-migrate, which answers 204; id 0 moves it back to the default project. The result is visible through POST /{id}/resources/filter. (KB-6OP2, live, checked 2026-06-03)
- The vendor's per-service Python SDK clients fail with region lookup errors when they are pointed at eu-de, because eu-de is missing from the region list those clients ship with. (KB-7LDG, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On 2026-09-25 a signed POST with an empty body reached request validation, not authentication, on ECS, VPC, EVS, RDS, ELB, CBR, CES, SMN, WAF, VPCEP, VPN, NAT and IMS in eu-de: each answered its own HTTP 400 error and none answered 401. The DRS job list and the KMS key list, both POST calls, answered 200, and signed creates and deletes on CES, WAF, VPC, ELB and ECS succeeded the same day. (KB-DK5M, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The Authorization header of a signed call has the form SDK-HMAC-SHA256 Access=<access key>, SignedHeaders=<names>, Signature=<hex>. The signed header names are lowercased, sorted and joined with semicolons. (KB-DVYO, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A call to a service endpoint in another region needs that region's own project_id. Using the eu-de project_id against an eu-nl endpoint returns HTTP 403, which reads like the service being absent in that region. (KB-FOCJ, live, checked 2026-08-27)
- A signed call carries an X-Sdk-Date header with the UTC timestamp in YYYYMMDDThhmmssZ form. The header name is part of the signed header list, so the signature covers the request timestamp. (KB-GLEX, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The DLI SQL check, POST /v1.0/{project_id}/jobs/check-sql, answers at once without submitting a job and already refuses unsupported DDL: on 2026-09-25 it refused CREATE TABLE ... USING ICEBERG with DLI.0007 and STORED BY with DLI.0005, so it tests what DLI supports without a queue run. (KB-HZUX, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In the canonical request each path segment is percent-encoded and the path ends with a slash, query parameters are sorted by key with key and value percent-encoded, and the body hash is SHA256 of the raw body, or of the empty string when there is no body. (KB-IMQR, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- In the vendor SDK on huaweicloudsdkcore 3.1.186, the region lookup <Service>Region.value_of("eu-de"), whose result .with_region() normally takes, raises KeyError because eu-de is not in the region table the SDK ships. A plain string passed to .with_region() raises nothing there, and build() then fails with AttributeError. (KB-LCLP, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- TCP API requests are authenticated with an AK/SK signature of type SDK-HMAC-SHA256. The algorithm needs only HMAC-SHA256 and SHA-256, so an HTTP client plus a standard crypto library can sign calls. (KB-LHNK, live, checked 2026-09-23)
- The enterprise project API supports list, create, query, update and enable/disable, and has no delete operation. The default enterprise project id is 0. (KB-LYSJ, live, checked 2026-06-03)
- A TCP API request signed with SDK-HMAC-SHA256 carries only three headers inside the signature: Host, X-Sdk-Date and Content-Type. The string to sign is SDK-HMAC-SHA256, the X-Sdk-Date value and the SHA256 of the canonical request, joined by newlines. (KB-M5D7, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- Service APIs on T Cloud Public (TCP) accept request signing with an access key and a secret key, of type SDK-HMAC-SHA256. The project id is not part of the signature: it is a segment of most service paths, and the path is signed as part of the canonical request. (KB-MH3Y, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- FunctionGraph functions are created with POST /v2/{project_id}/fgs/functions on functiongraph.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com. The code of an existing function is replaced with PUT on the same path plus /{urn}/code, runtime, memory and xrole are changed with PUT /{urn}/config, and a synchronous invoke is POST /{urn}/invocations with the event as body. (KB-MUMT, live, checked 2026-07-30)
- Postman substitutes {{variable}} as plain text, so a placeholder written inside quotes reaches the API as a JSON string. For a field typed Integer or Boolean the placeholder has to stand outside the quotes, as "size": {{root_disk_size}}. (KB-NBWO, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- A signature computed over a URL or body different from the one actually sent returns HTTP 401, and the response carries nothing that identifies the mismatch as the cause. (KB-NFGJ, live, checked 2026-08-18)
- Timestamp formats differ per service in listings: CCE returns "2026-09-16 16:14:34.249535 +0000 UTC", RDS "2026-09-13T14:13:41+0000", EIP create_time "2026-09-02 09:10:06" and VPC "2026-04-14T13:10:40" without a zone. (KB-POCS, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- The TCP API gateway distinguishes two 404 texts: "method X not found" means the path is registered with other methods only, "has not been published in the environment" means the path is unknown. /v2/zones/{id}/dnssec/enable, /rotate, /keys and /v2.1/zones/{id}/dnssec returned the second. (KB-Q4H2, live, checked 2026-09-10)
- In the canonical request of an SDK-HMAC-SHA256 signature the URL path is percent-encoded segment by segment and ends with a trailing slash, and the query parameters are sorted by name. (KB-R3TW, live, checked 2026-09-23)
- In otcextensions 0.32.29 the service list registers drs, vpn (Classic VPN, v2) and evpn (Enterprise VPN, v5), but their proxy modules are empty stubs without resource methods, so DRS and VPN still have to be called as raw signed REST requests. (KB-ROZI, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET /providers on the EPS API reports an unreliable total_count under a small limit: it returned 4 with limit=5 while the real total is 44 with limit=200. (KB-SPQQ, live, checked 2026-06-03)
- With code_type "inline" FunctionGraph expects base64 of the plain source file. A base64 zip is accepted at create and fails only at invoke with "failed to find module 'index' (invalid or missing encoding declaration)", which reads like a Python error. The Python runtime also wants the coding declaration on line 1. (KB-SQNC, live, checked 2026-07-30)
- The TMS predefined-tag API uses the URI /v1.0/predefine_tags on tms.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com; the spelling predefined-tags returned 404 APIGW.0101. POST /v1.0/predefine_tags/action with action create or delete returns HTTP 204. (KB-WICU, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- The EPS v1.0 API in eu-de answers GET /v1.0/enterprise-projects with 200 and the keys enterprise_projects and total_count, POST with 201 carrying enterprise_project.id, GET and PUT on /{id} with 200, and POST /{id}/action with action enable or disable with 204. (KB-WMI3, live, checked 2026-06-02)
- MRS Manager users, roles and groups can be scripted over https://<manager>:28443/web/api/v2/permission/ with HTTP basic auth. The request schemas are undocumented but readable on the node under /opt/Bigdata/om-server/apache-tomcat-*/webapps/web/WEB-INF/validate/. (KB-X6L3, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- The string to sign joins with newlines the literal SDK-HMAC-SHA256, the X-Sdk-Date value and the SHA-256 hex digest of the canonical request. The signature is HMAC-SHA256 over that string, keyed with the secret key itself. (KB-YYWK, live, checked 2026-09-23)
- GET /v1.0/{project_id}/databases returning 400 DLI.0999 'Only active cluster manager can create resource domain' means DLI is still unprovisioned, without an elastic resource pool, rather than an authentication failure. (KB-Z3SM, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- In the EPS resources/filter call the field region_id is mandatory when resource_type is bucket. (KB-ZF6E, docs, checked 2026-07-01)

## cbr (34)

- A crash-consistent restore of a Windows ECS backed up during continuous writes contained one truncated file out of 37, while a comparison run with writes stopped and the volume cache flushed before the backup contained zero damaged files out of 57. (KB-3PZR, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- Creating a CBR vault with consistent_level app_consistent returns HTTP 400 with error code BackupService.6126, while crash_consistent returns 200. The API enum accepts both values. Checked on a vault with object_type server. (KB-3T3I, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- CBR keeps backup copies inside its own vaults. The service overview constraints page lists download of a backup to a local PC and upload of a backup to OBS as operations the service excludes. Vault locking therefore protects only the copies held in CBR. (KB-47P2, docs, checked 2026-09-16)
- Removing a protected resource from a CBR vault lowers the vault's `allocated` value on its own. One vault went from 190 GB to 140 GB after a resource was removed, with only the removal call. (KB-4HSQ, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- A Linux ext4 root filesystem captured while the server was running failed to boot after restore: the clone superblock read clean with errors and ext4_orphan_get reported EFSCORRUPTED. Restored from a backup of the same server taken while stopped, it booted in 66 seconds with a clean superblock. (KB-6CUR, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A Linux server restored from an enhanced CBR backup reached service in 31 to 33 seconds independent of data volume: disk handed over in 6 to 7 seconds by Instant Restore, standby start 11 seconds, attach 12 seconds, mount 3 to 5 seconds. Instant Restore requires an enhanced backup, readable as support_lld. (KB-6DKR, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- The units in a CBR vault payload are mixed: `billing.size` and `billing.allocated` are GB, while `billing.used` is MB. Reading `billing.used` as GB turns 155588 MB into an apparent 155 TB. (KB-6IRS, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- After a CBR server restore a logical volume group has to be attached again, and the CBR FAQ covers a login password that became random after a restore or after creating a server from an image and points to the ECS password reset. (KB-73MN, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A cold rebuild from backup to a working service measured 111 s with a Debian guest and 149 to 168 s with Windows Server 2022. The Debian figure holds only for a backup taken from a stopped machine. Different phases and disk sizes, so an order of magnitude, not a controlled comparison. (KB-7DCC, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A volume restored from a CBR backup is usable at once through Instant Restore and loads its blocks lazily in the background, so full read speed comes later. This applies to every restored data volume, not only to the system volume of a machine rebuilt from an image. (KB-BNJP, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- CBR backups are created with consistent_level crash_consistent. A request for app_consistent is rejected by the service with error BackupService.6126. (KB-C2ML, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- When accepting a shared CBR backup, the member_id in the accept path is the destination project id. The member UUID returned in the share response is rejected with 404 BackupService.6705. (KB-C4AO, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Deleting a CBR vault that holds backups is asynchronous in eu-de: after DELETE /v3/{project_id}/vaults/{vault_id} the vault reported billing.status deleting, its used capacity dropped to 0 after about a minute and the vault answered 404 BackupService.6105 'Vault Not Found' after about three minutes. A vault without backups was gone at the first check. (KB-CNKG, live, checked 2026-10-05)
- A pilot light switchover (start the stopped standby, create one data volume from a CBR backup, attach it and bring it online) measured 31 to 33 s with a Debian guest and 66 s with a Windows Server 2022 guest. (KB-D5LC, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- Per the EVS user guide a disk created from a CBR backup is encrypted or not by a choice made at creation, while the EVS API reference says a create call without __system__encrypted gives the new disk the encryption attribute of its data source. A live restore of an encrypted volume on a test tenant in eu-de on 2026-02-04 produced a target volume with the encryption flag off. (KB-DESF, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- An EVS root volume restored from a CBR backup that was taken while the machine was running failed to boot in 3 of 3 attempts on Debian with ext4. The same path on Windows Server 2022 with NTFS booted, and chkdsk reported no errors. (KB-DKE3, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- The restore point of a CBR backup sits at the start of the backup run. A source counter that moved from 220 to 412 during a 304 s backup came back at 234 on the restored disk, so RPO counts from the backup start. (KB-E435, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A CBR vault keeps its resource binding after the bound ECS is deleted: on 2026-10-05 a server vault and a disk vault in eu-de still listed the server (OS::Nova::Server) and its volume (OS::Cinder::Volume) with protect_status available, while the ECS server listing no longer returned that server. A cleanup by resource therefore has to look at the vaults as well. (KB-EBMP, live, checked 2026-10-05)
- In a CBR vault, size is the configured capacity in GB, billing.allocated is the sum of the sizes of the protected resources rather than the vault capacity, and billing.used is in MB. (KB-EQOS, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- CBR vault backup locking keeps automatic backups locked until their retention period ends. It is described in the documentation mirror under Cloud Backup and Recovery vault management and is not named in the service description. (KB-HVJS, docs, checked 2026-09-16)
- CBR vault backup locking keeps automatic backups undeletable by any user for the whole retention period, stays enabled once switched on, leaves manual backups deletable, and still allows cross-region replication of the locked vault. (KB-I63S, docs, checked 2026-09-01)
- CBR vault creation documents crash_consistent as the consistency level value, and the CBR service overview describes CBR backups as crash-consistent. (KB-IJTE, docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) runs scheduled backups with a set retention period: a Cloud Backup and Recovery (CBR) backup policy runs on chosen weekdays or every 1 to 30 days at a set time and keeps backups by count or for one month, three months, six months, one year or a custom period of 2 to 99,999 days, with optional weekly, monthly and yearly long-term rules. RDS databases are backed up automatically every day by default and kept for 1 to 732 days. (KB-ITGL, docs, checked 2026-10-11)
- CBR vault backup locking keeps automatic backups locked until their retention period ends, giving a second native WORM option next to OBS Object Lock. It is documented in the CBR user guide under vault management, enabling backup locking. (KB-J2C5, docs, checked 2026-09-16)
- Restoring a CBR server backup stops the server and starts it again when the restore ends unless Start the server immediately after restoration is deselected, and the backup overwrites the current disks, by default the original ones or another disk of at least the same size. (KB-K6HK, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In a CBR vault object the billing.size field is the capacity in GB, while billing.used is the used capacity in MB (API reference: 'Used capacity, in MB'). A disk vault of size 10 with used 3084 holds about 3 GB, not more than its capacity. (KB-LGK7, docs, checked 2026-10-05)
- The TCP service description, as checked on 2026-09-16, names neither CBR backup locking nor OBS WORM. (KB-MC6B, contract, checked 2026-09-16)
- CBR backup deletion is asynchronous: delete calls return success while the backups move to state `deleting`, and a list call issued right after still counts all of them. They drain to zero in about 60 seconds. A resource removed from a vault sits at `protect_status: removing` for the same window. (KB-MYLM, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- A CBR vault created with POST /v3/{project_id}/vaults protects disks when billing.object_type is disk and the resource entries use type OS::Cinder::Volume. Whole-server protection uses the resource type OS::Nova::Server instead. (KB-PP2P, live, checked 2026-08-26)
- Encryption of a volume created from a CBR backup is decided when the volume is created, since per the EVS documentation the encryption attribute of a disk cannot be changed afterwards. For an encrypted backup shared into another tenant the CBR documentation also requires the key to be shared with the recipient. (KB-Q6ES, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A CBR backup of a Windows Server 2022 ECS taken while the guest wrote continuously to the NTFS system disk restored to a machine that booted, reported the dirty bit as NOT Dirty, logged a healthy volume event and passed a read-only chkdsk. (KB-SPKE, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- A CBR vault can be shrunk: PUT /v3/{project_id}/vaults/{vault_id} with {"vault":{"billing":{"size":300}}} returned 200, the vault reported 300 GB at once and the existing backups stayed intact. The new size has to stay above billing.used. (KB-TWYK, live, checked 2026-09-17)
- Cloud Backup and Recovery (CBR) provides backup and restore for Elastic Cloud Servers, Elastic Volume Service disks and SFS Turbo file systems: the customer backs up all or selected volumes of a server, sets the execution time of automated backups and their retention period, and can replicate server backups to the other region. Backups are stored in vaults, the first backup is full and later ones are incremental, and backup policies set timing, frequency and retention. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for CBR in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-Y4LS, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- CBR backups are incremental after the first one. A second backup of the same 81 GiB server took 243 seconds against 501 seconds for the first, which ran at roughly 3.5 seconds per GiB plus about 220 seconds fixed. (KB-YC6H, live, checked 2026-09-02)

## ces (33)

- In eu-de on 2026-09-25 the 4-hour and 24-hour rollups (period 14400 and 86400) reached back 235 days to the creation of the oldest measured resource, beyond the 155 days of the 1-hour rollups. The docs give no retention for these two periods, so whether Cloud Eye keeps a full year could not be proven. (KB-2FSY, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de a CES resource group is created with POST /V1.0/{project_id}/resource-groups. The body carries group_name and a resources array, where each entry names a namespace such as SYS.ECS and a dimensions list with a name like instance_id and the resource id as value. (KB-3GQL, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de the CES v2 call for a single resource group returned only the group metadata, without the resources list that the V1.0 path returned for the same group. (KB-4475, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET V1.0/{project_id}/resource-groups/{group_id} returns the group together with its full resources array. The v2 path for the same group returns only the group metadata. (KB-4Y5B, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de the same resource group create request sent to the CES v2 path returned HTTP 400, while the V1.0 path accepted it. (KB-54E2, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- CES paths written with a lowercase v1 version segment return HTTP 404 in TCP eu-de. The version segment that resolves is the capital-V form V1.0. (KB-5OSP, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A CES resource group is created with POST /V1.0/{project_id}/resource-groups, which answered HTTP 201 on 2026-09-25. The v2 resource-groups path takes its own body (group_name, type, tags) and answered HTTP 200 the same day, while a V1.0-shaped body sent to v2 is refused with HTTP 400 ces.0014 "Some content in message body is not correct". (KB-6AI5, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de the members of a CES resource group are read with GET /V1.0/{project_id}/resource-groups/{group_id}. The response returns them under the key resources, each entry carrying namespace, dimensions as name and value pairs, and status. (KB-6HWY, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A CES alarm rule references its resources by ID and its notification target by SMN topic, so the resource group members and the SMN topic exist before the alarm rule is created. CES dimensions carry resource IDs, so ECS and RDS names are resolved to IDs first. (KB-74D3, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Pushed data is read back with GET /V1.0/{project_id}/metric-data and the parameters namespace, metric_name, dim.0 to dim.2, from, to, period (1, 300, 1200, 3600, 14400 or 86400) and filter. On 2026-09-25 this GET answered HTTP 200 with datapoints on ces.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com for the system metric SYS.ECS cpu_util. (KB-BUCK, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In TCP eu-de on 2026-09-25 GET /v2/{project_id}/alarms lists the CES alarms of a project under alarms with a count, each with name, type, namespace, policies and resources. GET /V1.0/{project_id}/alarms lists the same alarms under metric_alarms. (KB-CBMO, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The CES metrics list path is case sensitive. Written with capital V1.0 it returns the metric list, written as v1 the API gateway answers HTTP 404. (KB-E3AA, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de, CES creates an alarm with POST /V1.0/{project_id}/alarms on ces.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com, the version segment written with an uppercase V. The body carries metric (namespace such as SYS.ECS, dimensions, metric_name), condition, alarm_type and alarm_enabled. (KB-F3UI, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- An application adds its own metric data with POST /V1.0/{project_id}/metric-data on the Cloud Eye endpoint: the body is an array of entries, each with metric (namespace, metric_name and up to three dimensions), ttl of 1 to 604,800 seconds, collect_time in UNIX milliseconds and value, plus optional unit and type. The call answers 201 without a body, while each data point is deleted once its ttl expires. (KB-FEHS, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Cloud Eye (CES) monitors the status of cloud resources in real time and raises alarms: monitoring starts automatically for resources such as Elastic Cloud Servers, the customer sets alarm rules on metrics and views them on dashboards. The documentation adds an agent for Elastic Cloud Servers that collects about 40 metrics such as CPU, memory and disk usage at 60-second granularity, and alarm notifications by email, SMS or HTTP/HTTPS. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for Cloud Eye in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-FK5P, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- In eu-de on 2026-09-25 the Cloud Eye metric-data API returned raw data back to 2 days, 5-minute rollups back to 10 days, 20-minute rollups back to 20 days and 1-hour rollups back to 155 days, so the 5-minute rollups lived longer than the documented 6 days. (KB-K6SW, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A metric-data POST may not exceed 512 KB and every collect_time must lie between three days before and ten minutes after the current time, with 20 seconds of margin at each end. Data should be sent more often than the aggregation period, otherwise the rollups get gaps. (KB-KUTY, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) monitors resources and raises alarms with Cloud Eye (CES): metrics of cloud services are collected automatically, an agent adds operating system metrics for servers, alarm rules on thresholds trigger notifications through Simple Message Notification topics by email, SMS or HTTP/HTTPS, and dashboards show the data. Application-level monitoring comes from Application Operations Management and Application Performance Management. (KB-L4FW, docs, checked 2026-10-11)
- A CES metric listing in eu-de on 2026-09-25 returned about 1520 metrics, the count moving slightly between reads, in the namespaces SYS.ECS, SYS.ELB, SYS.EVS, SYS.FunctionGraph, SYS.NAT, SYS.OBS, SYS.RDS and SYS.VPC. No SYS.CCE namespace was among them. (KB-M42H, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In a CES alarm body, alarm_type RESOURCE_GROUP targets a resource group and its dimensions carry names without values, MULTI_INSTANCE targets named instances and its dimensions carry name and value, EVENT.SYS marks a system event alarm whose dimensions can be empty. (KB-MLR6, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The Cloud Eye docs give retention periods of two days for raw metric data, 6 days for 5-minute rollups, 20 days for 20-minute rollups and 155 days for 1-hour rollups. The console lets you pick a custom time range of up to six months. (KB-OSCN, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Cloud Eye can dump cloud service monitoring data in real time to a DMS for Kafka topic, where it is read on the Kafka console or with an open-source Kafka client. An account can create at most 20 dump tasks and Kafka is the only destination type. (KB-P2MX, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Custom metrics show up in the Cloud Eye console under Custom Monitoring once data has been added through the API. Alarm rules can be created on them from that page. (KB-QDU7, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A CES alarm carries its notification targets in alarm_actions and ok_actions, entries of type notification whose notificationList holds an SMN topic URN. The V1.0 body also sets alarm_action_enabled; a notification time window is documented only in the v2 alarm API, as notification_begin_time and notification_end_time. (KB-REPW, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In TCP eu-de the CES v2 metric list and the v2 single alarm GET return HTTP 404, while V1.0 serves both. Resource group creation works on both paths, each with its own body (KB-6AI5). (KB-SNTC, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- CES metric listing runs on the V1.0 path: GET /V1.0/{project_id}/metrics with limit 1000 answered HTTP 200 and returns meta_data.total. CES alarm calls use the same V1.0 path. (KB-TMYE, live, checked 2026-07-02)
- A CES alarm is bound to a resource group through the field metric.resource_group_id in the alarm body. (KB-TO6W, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET /V1.0/{project_id}/resource-groups/{group_id} returns the members of a CES resource group in a resources array, each entry carrying the metric namespace and the dimensions of the resource. (KB-UI56, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The CES metric list is returned by GET /V1.0/{project_id}/metrics with a capital V in the version segment. The same path written with a lowercase v returns HTTP 404. (KB-VFOS, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On a test tenant in T Cloud Public (TCP) eu-de on 2026-09-25 the v2 and V1.0 CES alarm lists returned the same two alarm ids. (KB-W3FA, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de CES alarms are listed with GET /V1.0/{project_id}/alarms, where the version segment is the literal string V1.0. The response returns metric_alarms plus a meta_data object with total and marker, and the marker value is sent back as the start parameter to fetch the next page. (KB-X4W2, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- POST /v2/{project_id}/alarm-templates creates a custom CES alarm template, HTTP 201 on 2026-09-25. Its batch delete needs delete_associate_alarm in the body next to template_ids: without it the call answers HTTP 400 ces.0014. (KB-XQ7F, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- CES alarm rules are created with POST /V1.0/{project_id}/alarms and listed with GET /V1.0/{project_id}/alarms. In eu-de on 2026-09-25 GET /v2/{project_id}/alarms listed the same alarms as the V1.0 path. (KB-YCXE, live, checked 2026-09-25)

## waf (28)

- The CloudWAF release note of 20 March 2025 says it only works with a dedicated load balancer created on or after 27 January 2025. (KB-2MGI, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Dedicated WAF has resources dedicated per instance, can be spread over up to three availability zones and is reached through an internet-facing ELB. It comes as Professional WI-100 with 100 Mbit/s and 2,000 QPS or Enterprise WI-500 with 500 Mbit/s and 10,000 QPS, each with 60,000 WAF-to-server connections. (KB-2SIL, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- When a protected domain is added in Cloud WAF with POST /v1/{project_id}/waf/instance, the certificate is referenced by the field certificate_id in snake_case. The spellings certid, cert_id and certificateid each returned WAF.1001 "invalid request". (KB-5A7O, live, checked 2026-05-26)
- From 1 October 2026 Classic Mode gets no regular updates, patches or bug fixes. Technical support continues on a best-effort basis without any obligation to provide bug fixes or security patches and continued operation is at the customer's own risk. (KB-BKQH, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- The table in section 3.9.6 gives Cloud Mode the same metrics as Classic Mode: 300 Mbit/s, 10,000 QPS and 6,000 WAF-to-server connections per domain name. Classic Mode is connected by a CNAME DNS record change, while the access mode cell via internet-facing ELB spans both the Dedicated and the Cloud rows. (KB-BLS4, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- Web Application Firewall (Dedicated) (WAF) is a web application firewall whose resources are dedicated per instance: it monitors, filters and blocks HTTP and HTTPS traffic to web servers with customer-set policies and logging, can be spread over up to three availability zones and is reached through an internet-facing Elastic Load Balancer. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for dedicated WAF in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-CE5J, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- WAF events are listed with GET /v1/{project_id}/waf/event on waf.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com, with the from and to parameters given in epoch milliseconds; the API reference pages with offset and limit (and marker), while a live test on 2026-02-04 used pageindex and pagesize. The response key is items next to total, and each event holds id, host, url, rule, action, sip, payload and time. (KB-D4HX, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- After a dedicated WAF domain is deleted, the engine port named WAF_ENGINE_<id> with device_owner compute:<az> stays ACTIVE in its subnet. While it is there, deleting the security group returns 409 VPC.0602 SecurityGroupInUse and deleting the subnet returns 409 VPC.0209. (KB-ELHG, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- Dedicated WAF accepts POST /v1/{project_id}/waf/policy without a WAF instance in eu-de (HTTP 200) and deletes such a policy with HTTP 204. A global protection whitelist rule on such a policy was refused with WAF.1001 on 2026-09-25, even with the documented example body. (KB-GA75, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A WAF ignore rule for a false alarm needs the event_id field in the request body next to the path; the API reference marks both as mandatory and gives the rule back only in the response. A body sent without event_id was answered with HTTP 400 Bad request in a live test on 2026-02-04. (KB-JADS, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Web Application Firewall (Cloud Mode) (WAF) monitors, filters and blocks HTTP and HTTPS traffic to web servers; the customer creates, configures and monitors it, activates protection policies and logs their activity. The documentation names protection against SQL injection, cross-site scripting, web shells, command and code injection, file inclusion, CC attacks, malicious crawlers and CSRF, and a Cloud Mode instance provides the ELB access mode, in which WAF inspects traffic inside the gateway of a dedicated application load balancer (KB-TJGQ). Cloud Mode is generally available in eu-de and eu-nl since 3 March 2026 (KB-KFC4). (KB-K346, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- Cloud Mode (CloudWAF) is generally available in EU-DE and EU-NL without whitelisting since 3 March 2026. Cloud Mode and Dedicated Mode are both created and managed in the console under Web Application Firewall. (KB-KFC4, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) offers a web application firewall as Web Application Firewall in Cloud Mode and in Dedicated Mode: it monitors, filters and blocks HTTP and HTTPS traffic to web servers with customer-set protection policies and logs its activity. Dedicated Mode has its own resources per instance across up to three availability zones and is reached through an internet-facing load balancer. (KB-KIAP, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- DELETE /v1/{project_id}/waf/instance/{domain_id}?keepPolicy=false returns 204 and removes the WAF domain together with its bound policy. A separate delete of that policy afterwards answers 404 WAF.3001. (KB-KJ6E, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- POST /v1/{project_id}/waf/certificate on waf.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com returns WAF.1605 "invalid certificate content format" for a well-formed multi-line PEM. With every newline removed from the certificate content and from the private key, the same request returns 200 and a certificate id. (KB-KYXU, live, checked 2026-05-26)
- On 2026-09-25 the Classic WAF API on waf.eu-de still answered GET /v1/{project_id}/waf/instance. The dedicated WAF API answers on its own endpoint premium-waf.eu-de and its path /v1/{project_id}/premium-waf/instance is not served on waf.eu-de. (KB-LAFN, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- WAF Dedicated host create names the same certificate reference certificateid, without the underscore, so a request body that works for Cloud WAF carries the wrong field name for Dedicated. (KB-MNON, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On eu-de no tenant API removes a dedicated WAF engine after its domain has been deleted. (KB-NAYN, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- An ec-256 ECDSA certificate returned WAF.1605 from the Cloud WAF certificate upload even after the newlines were stripped, while an RSA certificate sent the same way was accepted. (KB-NQ7B, live, checked 2026-05-26)
- The WAF API reports the protection state of a domain in the field protect_status, where 0 means protection is off, 1 means protection is on and -1 means WAF is bypassed. (KB-OJXA, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The successors of Classic Mode are Cloud Mode and Dedicated Mode. The service description names Cloud Mode as its strategic replacement and the release notes of 1 June 2026 recommend starting the migration now. (KB-ORQX, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- A WAF ignore rule is created with POST /v1/{project_id}/waf/policy/{policy_id}/ignore on waf.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com. The API reference lists path and event_id as the body fields, both required, and returns rule, event_type and the id of the new masking rule in the response. (KB-OX3H, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Web Application Firewall - Modes and Metrics (WAFMM) sets the access modes and capacity of the WAF offers: the classic shared mode is connected by a CNAME DNS record change, while the access mode cell via internet-facing ELB spans the Dedicated and Cloud rows (KB-BLS4). Cloud Mode handles 300 Mbit/s, 10,000 QPS and 6,000 WAF-to-server connections per domain name; Dedicated Mode comes as Professional WI-100 (100 Mbit/s, 2,000 QPS) or Enterprise WI-500 (500 Mbit/s, 10,000 QPS), each with 60,000 WAF-to-server connections. (KB-PMPA, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- The WAF event list endpoint /v1/{project_id}/waf/event takes the from and to window as epoch milliseconds and pages its result with offset and limit per the API reference (pageindex and pagesize were used in a live test on 2026-02-04). The event_id that an ignore rule requires is taken from this list. (KB-SQUD, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Event IDs for a WAF ignore rule come from `GET /v1/{project_id}/waf/event?from={ms}&to={ms}`, which takes the time window as millisecond timestamps. (KB-SVH6, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A Cloud Mode instance provides the ELB access mode of WAF: WAF is built into the gateway of a dedicated load balancer with an application (HTTP/HTTPS) specification, inspects the traffic there and does not forward it itself. (KB-TJGQ, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- WAF Classic Mode is no longer bookable from 1 June 2026 and reaches End-of-Maintenance and Support on 1 October 2026. (KB-WNNJ, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- The WAF ignore rule call POST /v1/{project_id}/waf/policy/{policy_id}/ignore requires path and event_id in the body per the API reference, which lists rule only as a response field. A body without event_id returned HTTP 400 Bad Request in a live test on 2026-02-04. (KB-YECU, docs, checked 2026-09-25)

## gpu (27)

- On 2026-09-25 the eu-de public image catalog held 57 images flagged for GPU flavors, 45 of them Linux, and only six Linux ones name an NVIDIA driver in their description: Standard_Ubuntu_22.04_GPU_NV, latest and prev, with Nvidia public driver included, and Standard_RockyLinux_9_GRID in bios and uefi variants with the GRID driver file NVIDIA-Linux-x86_64-535.288.01-grid.run included. (KB-3JIJ, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A 700B-class open MoE model in AWQ-INT4, served with the vllm/vllm-openai container on p5e.40xlarge.12 with eight H100 NVL cards, reached about 36 tokens per second with 0.11 s time to first token end to end. (KB-3QWJ, live, checked 2026-05-08)
- DJI Terra ships no official CLI or headless API. The backend engines DJIMapGen.exe, TerraWorkingProcess.exe, terra_3dgs.exe and DJIPicMapGen.exe are driven from an Electron GUI (DJIBrowser.exe). (KB-4A7E, docs, checked 2026-06-23)
- The NVIDIA Container Toolkit install guide requires an installed NVIDIA GPU driver and a supported container engine such as Docker, then the toolkit packages, and for Docker the command nvidia-ctk runtime configure --runtime=docker, which updates /etc/docker/daemon.json so that Docker can use the NVIDIA Container Runtime, followed by a restart of the Docker daemon. (KB-4C3N, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On an eight-GPU instance, docker rm -f followed immediately by docker run --gpus all sometimes gives the new container only seven GPUs while nvidia-smi -L on the host still lists eight, and torch aborts with device >= 0 && device < num_gpus. Restarting the docker daemon between swaps restored all eight. (KB-5GI3, live, checked 2026-05-07)
- vLLM 0.19.1 loading AWQ-INT4 weights through the compressed-tensors MarlinLinearKernel path repacks the layout in memory at load, about 110 seconds per 5 GB shard, single threaded per worker on cold page cache (81 shards, about 2.5 hours). Raising OMP_NUM_THREADS slowed it to about 200 seconds per shard on 80 vCPU. (KB-65QI, live, checked 2026-05-07)
- GPU flavors in eu-de come with fixed RAM, and every Windows GPU flavor cheaper than g6.10xlarge.7 on pay-as-you-go has 32 to 128 GB RAM (2026-09-25). g6.10xlarge.7 itself pairs one T4 with 280 GB RAM, and p2s.8xlarge.8 and p2v.8xlarge.8 reach 256 GB with only 32 vCPU. (KB-CQ2S, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The p5s GPU flavors carry H100 PCIe cards, not H100 NVL, so their GPU topology differs from p5e. (KB-CSNR, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The p5e.40xlarge.12 flavor (8x H100 NVL, 94 GB each) links its GPUs as NVLink bridge pairs without an NVSwitch fabric, so traffic between pairs goes over PCIe. nvidia-fabricmanager installs but fails to start there with 'Detected Pre-NVL5 system', leaving a broken systemd unit, so leave it out. (KB-DPSI, live, checked 2026-05-07)
- The flavor g6.10xlarge.7 in eu-de provides one T4 with 16 GB (about 15 GB usable framebuffer), 40 vCPU from 20 physical Xeon Gold 6266 cores at 3.0 GHz with hyper-threading, 280 GB RAM and dedicated CPU. (KB-F3C3, live, checked 2026-06-23)
- No H100 GPU flavor is available in eu-nl. On 2026-09-25 the ECS flavor list of eu-nl held no p5s or p5e flavor and no flavor with an H100 GPU, while eu-de listed four p5s and three p5e flavors, all in eu-de-01. (KB-IWD5, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The p5s flavor carries H100 PCIe cards, a different GPU topology from the p5e H100 NVL flavors. (KB-JLBD, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- p5e.40xlarge.12 provides eight H100 NVL cards of 94 GB, wired as NVLink bridge pairs with no NVSwitch fabric, so cross-pair traffic falls back to PCIe. nvidia-fabricmanager installs but its unit fails to start and leaves a broken systemd unit behind. (KB-JRCC, live, checked 2026-05-07)
- Reserved terms of 12, 24 and 36 months are offered for the GPU flavor p5e.10xlarge.12 in eu-de, each also as an upfront-payment variant. (KB-KWII, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- GPU flavors, pi5e included, keep billing compute while the instance is stopped. The pricing-models documentation is the billing-authoritative source; per-family spec notes stating the opposite are unreliable. (KB-MBIY, docs, checked 2026-07-02)
- In eu-de on 2026-09-25, p2s.16xlarge.8 (eight V100 of 32 GB, 64 vCPU, 512 GB RAM, end announced for 31.12.2026) and pi5e.24xlarge.4 (six L4 of 24 GB, 96 vCPU, 384 GB RAM) are orderable and take Windows Server images, so g7.12xlarge.8 (one A40 of 48 GB) is not the largest Windows-capable GPU flavor. g6.20xlarge.7 still offers two T4, 80 vCPU and 560 GB RAM. (KB-NIV7, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- DJI Terra is documented as compatible with NVIDIA graphics cards only, with a minimum of 4 GB VRAM and 32 GB RAM and a recommendation of an RTX 2070 with 64 GB RAM. (KB-QATO, docs, checked 2026-06-17)
- Section 3.1.1.2 of the TCP service description (revision 17.08.2026) marks the H100 flavor families p5s (NVIDIA H100) and p5e (NVIDIA H100 NVL) as only in EU-DE, and section 6.2.1.13 shows n.a. in every EU-NL column of all p5s and p5e rows. (KB-QLMG, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- A DJI Terra LiDAR reconstruction on g6.10xlarge.7 peaked at about 127 GB working set (about 135 GB including the OS) and about 3 GB of the 15 GB framebuffer, with GPU utilisation mostly low. The RAM peak is dataset-bound and stayed the same at 1, 3 and 4 computing cores. (KB-TVQH, live, checked 2026-06-23)
- On 2026-09-25 in eu-de-01 only p5s.20xlarge.12 showed the status normal, while p5s.5xlarge.12, p5s.10xlarge.12, p5s.40xlarge.12 and all three p5e flavors showed sellout. (KB-USSN, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On the same dataset and flavor the run took 15.6 h with one computing core, 9.1 h with three and 9.1 h with four, so three cores cut about 40% and a fourth core changed nothing. About 4 h of all-core compute is fixed in every run. (KB-VECE, live, checked 2026-06-23)
- The GPU flavor p5e.10xlarge.12 provides two H100 NVL cards with 94 GB memory per card, 188 GB in total, with 40 vCPU and 480 GB RAM. Storage is not part of the flavor: the system disk and any EVS disks are separate resources. (KB-VZW3, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The ECS flavor p5e.40xlarge.12 in eu-de-01 provides eight H100 NVL cards, 752 GB of GPU memory in total. (KB-W4HV, live, checked 2026-05-06)
- None of the 139 public images in eu-de on 2026-09-25 mentions Docker, containerd, Podman, a container runtime or the NVIDIA Container Toolkit in its name or description. (KB-WCSR, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The ECS user guide says a GPU driver has to be installed before a GPU-accelerated ECS is used, that a P-series ECS from a Windows public image has a Tesla driver installed by default and that one from a Linux public image has none and needs the driver installed after creation. (KB-WYE4, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- pi-series GPU flavors under Windows expose the card in TCC mode, so CUDA compute runs but a GPU-rendered desktop does not. (KB-XAD4, live, checked 2026-06-11)
- On GPU passthrough instances under Windows Server, the NVIDIA R570 branch driver 573.48 works, while 580.88 and 596.36 produce Code 43. That branch tops out at CUDA 12.8. (KB-Z2DO, live, checked 2026-06-11)

## kms (25)

- A KMS grant of the default EVS key evs/default to another tenant was rejected, while the same grant on a custom KMS key was accepted. (KB-2RBG, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The console offers RSAES_OAEP_SHA_256 as wrapping algorithm while the get-parameters-for-import API also lists RSAES_PKCS1_V1_5 and RSAES_OAEP_SHA_1. The downloaded wrapping key expires after 24 hours. (KB-4WN4, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A KMS grant on the default EVS key evs/default is refused, while the same grant on a user created KMS key is accepted. (KB-7M7Y, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Symmetric imported material can be deleted by hand or given an expiry time (when expiry is chosen the console default is 24 hours after import). KMS deletes expired material within 24 hours while the key and its metadata stay in status Pending import and data encrypted with it cannot be decrypted until the same material is imported again. (KB-C3TF, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In eu-de the KMS call POST /v1.0/{project_id}/kms/list-key-detail does not exist (HTTP 404 APIGW.0101). POST /v1.0/{project_id}/kms/list-keys with a limit in the body returns both the key ids and key_details, including creation_date in milliseconds, so it serves as the detailed listing. (KB-DMLV, live, checked 2026-10-05)
