- SMN HTTP and HTTPS subscriptions accept only public network URLs and public IP addresses, so SMN cannot call a webhook on a private VPC address directly. Besides HTTP and HTTPS the user guide lists email, SMS and FunctionGraph (function) as protocols. (KB-ACTM, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Deleting an SMN subscription and creating it again sets its confirmation state back to unconfirmed. (KB-D6RJ, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- SMN delivers asynchronously without a timeliness guarantee: for an unreachable endpoint it queues the message and tries six more times, at intervals that depend on the queue length, then drops it without telling the publisher. The publish call only returns a message_id and a request_id. (KB-F6ZA, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- An SMN subscription confirmation token stays valid for 48 hours. (KB-FRLE, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- According to the SMN user guide, Cloud Eye and Anti-DDoS may publish to the topics of all users by default, while other services such as OBS have to be selected in the topic policy, and the services on offer there vary by region. (KB-LSZZ, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Simple Message Notification (SMN) is a scalable, event-driven notification service: the customer defines topics and sends messages to their subscribers manually or automatically by email, text message (SMS) or HTTP/HTTPS. It is used on its own or together with Cloud Eye, Cloud Trace Service and Anti-DDoS. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for SMN in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-M6UB, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- Per the SMN user guide, an HTTP or HTTPS subscription is confirmed when the subscriber visits the subscribe_url that SMN sends in a SubscriptionConfirmation message, valid for 48 hours. An email subscription is confirmed by opening the link in the confirmation mail, which is also valid for 48 hours. (KB-PPUL, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET /v2/{project_id}/notifications/topics on the SMN endpoint answers HTTP 200 and returns topic_count together with the topic list. (KB-QE47, live, checked 2026-07-02)
- SMN sends email notifications from one fixed no-reply address of the platform domain (noreply at otc.t-systems.com), and a topic display name only sets the sender name shown in front of that address. (KB-SS56, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In eu-de on 2026-09-25, GET /v2/{project_id}/notifications/topics/{topic_urn}/attributes?name=access_policy returned an empty access_policy for all three topics of a test project, which matches the documented default, so a topic grants OBS nothing until such a policy is set. (KB-V6YD, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Through the SMN API the authorization is a topic policy set with PUT /v2/{project_id}/notifications/topics/{topic_urn}/attributes/access_policy, policy version 2016-09-07, with a statement whose Principal is Service obs in lower case and whose Action includes SMN:Publish. The documented sample also grants SMN:QueryTopicDetail. (KB-YRKR, docs, checked 2026-09-25)

## dli (14)

- On POST /v3/{project_id}/elastic-resource-pools, DLI requires max_cu to equal min_cu. A pool with min 16 and max 64 returned DLI.0035 about insufficient CU quota, while 16/16 with the basic spec label returned HTTP 200 and reached AVAILABLE in roughly ten minutes. (KB-2NIV, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- Creating a DLI elastic resource pool requires max_cu equal to min_cu: 16/64 returns DLI.0035 while 16/16 returns HTTP 200. The pool reaches AVAILABLE in roughly ten minutes. (KB-3XJB, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- GET /v1.0/{project_id}/queues on DLI in eu-de showed the default queue reporting support_spark_versions of 2.4.5 on the date of the check. Queues are created with POST /v1.0/{project_id}/queues, and DELETE /v1.0/{project_id}/queues/{name} returned HTTP 200. (KB-4XEJ, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- In eu-de a general DLI queue listed support_spark_versions 2.4.5 and 3.1.1 with default_spark_version 2.4.5, while the default sql queue listed 2.4.5 only. A submit using Spark 3.3.1 was refused, so these fields report a default rather than everything a submit accepts. (KB-5ENU, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- On the shared default DLI queue statements sit in LAUNCHING for 15 minutes and longer, and the submit call gives up with DLI.0019 "Get sync job result timeout". The job id remains recoverable from that error text. (KB-7NI7, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- On a dedicated DLI queue in eu-de, an Iceberg table creation is rejected with "DLI.0007: Only support 'USING' PARQUET/ORC/JSON/CSV/HUDI/DELTA for create non dli table", while Parquet and ORC controls pass in the same run. HUDI and DELTA are accepted although the documentation lists only CSV, Parquet, ORC, JSON and Avro. (KB-D554, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- Data Lake Insight (DLI) is a serverless big data query and analysis service compatible with Apache Spark and Apache Flink that runs SQL statements and Spark applications on heterogeneous data sources. Jobs run on non-dedicated queues, which take resources only while a job runs, or on dedicated queues that run around the clock until deleted. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the DLI API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-D6ZP, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- A DLI elastic resource pool needs a minimum CU strictly greater than the sum of the minimum CU of the queues registered in it. A 16 CU pool therefore rejects a 16 CU queue while another queue is still registered. (KB-HNQS, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- GET /v1.0/{pid}/queues without a filter returned only the default sql queue in eu-de. General queues appeared through GET /v1.0/{pid}/queues?queue_type=general, GET /v3/{pid}/elastic-resource-pools/{pool}/queues or GET /v1.0/{pid}/queues/{name}. (KB-JYDX, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- PUT /v3/{pid}/elastic-resource-pools/{name} resizing a DLI elastic resource pool to 64 CU returned DLI.0035, insufficient resources, in eu-de. (KB-MLOK, live, checked 2026-07-29)
- POST /v1.0/{project_id}/jobs/submit-job on DLI is synchronous. On a congested queue it gives up with HTTP 408 and DLI.0019 "Get sync job result timeout with id", but the job id in that message is a real job that can still be polled at /jobs/{id}/status. (KB-NXZH, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- On DLI, POST /v1.0/{project_id}/databases returns HTTP 200 on a tenant that has not used the service before, while GET /v1.0/{project_id}/databases can return HTTP 400 with DLI.0999 on such an unprovisioned tenant. (KB-PTKT, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- GET /v1.0/{project_id}/jobs with limit and sort=start_time_desc returns DLI job outcomes, including a message field that carries the SQL error text. It is the reliable way to read a job result after a synchronous submit times out. (KB-PXT2, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- A DLI job reads Iceberg tables when the Iceberg jar is supplied with the job itself. (KB-WBCZ, live, checked 2026-07-30)

## security (14)

- Section 6.10.6 of the TCP service description (revision 17.08.2026) lists DBSS Basic, Pro and Advanced for EU-DE only (n.a. for EU-NL), while section 6.10.5 (dedicated WAF Professional and Enterprise), section 6.10.7 (HSS Enterprise, Premium, WTP and Container editions) and section 6.10.9 (Cloud Firewall) list both EU-DE and EU-NL. (KB-22YR, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- Under the ErgB-AV for T Cloud Public of April 2026 the contracting company acts as subprocessor when a customer processes personal data on behalf of its own customers, with the approved subprocessors of the platform listed in Appendix IV. GDPR Article 28(2) lets such a customer engage another processor only with the prior specific or general written authorisation of its controllers and, under a general authorisation, requires it to inform them of any intended addition or replacement of other processors. (KB-3EX6, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET /v1/resource-manager/policy-definitions returned 87 built-in policy definitions for eu-de on that date, among them required-tag-check, required-all-tags, required-tag-exist, resource-tag-not-empty, ecs-instance-no-public-ip, elb-loadbalancers-no-public-ip, rds-instance-no-public-ip and eip-unbound-check. (KB-5TJI, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- A Config/RMS run-evaluation call is accepted with HTTP 202 and the per-resource Compliant or NonCompliant results appear in policy-states about one to two minutes later, covering every project in the domain. (KB-6MGX, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- A Config policy assignment is written with PUT /v1/resource-manager/domains/{domain_id}/policy-assignments and requires the resource recorder to be enabled, otherwise the call returns 400 RMS.00010091. POST on the assignment's policy-states/run-evaluation returns HTTP 202 and per-resource Compliant or NonCompliant states appear after one to two minutes. (KB-AXSS, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- The built-in Config/RMS policy resource-tag-not-empty takes no parameters, while required-tag-check requires both a tag key and a tag value. An assignment is scoped by provider, resource type and region. (KB-FVUY, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- BSI C5:2020 Type 2 attestation is held for the platform and re-attested at least every 12 months. (KB-GEDK, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In TCP eu-de, GET /v1/{project_id}/secrets on kms.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com answers HTTP 404 APIGW.0101 like an unknown path and a host csms.eu-de has no DNS record, although the service description of 17.08.2026 lists Cloud Secret Management Service (CSMS) in section 3.9.9, with an eu-de entry in section 6.10.8. (KB-GUHJ, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Config/RMS in eu-de provides 87 built-in policies, among them required-tag and no-public-ip rules. A resource recorder plus a policy assignment returns per-resource compliance verdicts. (KB-HGAP, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- DBSS is not offered in eu-nl. On 2026-09-25 the DBSS audit API answered 404 APIGW.0101 in eu-nl but reached the service in eu-de (403 for a user without DBSS rights), and the public price API had DBSS records only for eu-de. (KB-JM53, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- BSI IT-Grundschutz certification is still in progress on 2026-09-25: filed 2025-07-28, on the BSI list of ongoing procedures as BSI-IGZ-0757, audit phase planned from January 2026, certificate expected in Q4 2026. The listing is a pending procedure, not a certificate, and the BSI list says nothing about tenders. (KB-NZRC, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A DNS probe on 2026-07-02 found that the SCM and CCM service endpoint names for eu-de do not resolve, so no managed certificate service endpoint answered in eu-de. (KB-OK4D, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- HSS ransomware prevention (Premium, WTP and Container editions) is host-agent detection and response with honeypot files and an optional server backup to a CBR vault, so HSS itself gives detection, not immutability; write-once (WORM) backups come from the separate CBR vault setting backup locking, which covers only automatic backups. On Windows the Premium and WTP editions add a self-protection policy, active when at least two of antivirus, HIPS and ransomware protection are on, that stops HSS processes from being ended and the agent from being uninstalled on the server; the docs list it for Windows only (checked 2026-09-25). (KB-QWTI, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In the T Cloud Public (TCP) contractual service description the product name Anti-DDoS is written with the Unicode hyphen U+2010 instead of the ASCII hyphen-minus. A text search of the extracted document text for the ASCII spelling returns zero hits. (KB-ZPJG, contract, checked 2026-09-08)

## deh (11)

- In eu-de the Dedicated Host listing GET /v1.0/{project_id}/dedicated-hosts answers HTTP 200 on a tenant that owns no host, returning an empty dedicated_hosts array and total 0. (KB-26NM, live, checked 2026-07-20)
- Host types per availability zone are read with GET /v1.0/{project_id}/availability-zone/{az}/dedicated-host-types. The response carries the type names only, so vCPU and memory sizes have to come from another source such as the price API. (KB-5GCH, live, checked 2026-07-20)
- Dedicated host (DeH) provides isolated physical servers that are fully dedicated to one customer and run that customer's Elastic Cloud Servers. Dedicated hosts come in general purpose, dedicated general purpose, memory optimized and ultra-high I/O types and are provisioned, monitored and managed in the console. Customer-owned Microsoft Windows Server and SQL Server licenses (bring your own license) may be used on physical and dedicated hosts. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for DeH in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-6VVI, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- On 2026-07-20 the Dedicated Host type listing returned for eu-de-01: s2, s2-medium, c3, c4, c7n, m3, m4, m7n; for eu-de-02: s2, s2-medium, c3, c4, c7n, m3; for eu-de-03: s2, s2-medium, s7n, c4, c7n. (KB-ESW2, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The per-VM OS license rows of the eu-de DeH listing (productSection=licence, key prefix deh.lic) covered Open Linux, Oracle Linux, RedHat, Suse, SUSE-SAP and Windows. (KB-FJ6E, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- For a Dedicated Host deployment the billed items are the host itself, attached EVS, EIP and the per-VM OS license row. ECS instances placed on the host are covered by the host charge, which makes the model usable with customer-owned OS licenses. (KB-PEKS, docs, checked 2026-07-20)
- The public price API accepts sn=deh and returns Dedicated Host records under response.result.deh, split into productSection=main host rows and productSection=licence per-VM license rows. priceAmount is an hourly value, R12 and R24 are flat monthly values, and the 36-month reserved fields carried no rate on any DeH row of the eu-de listing. (KB-T5E6, live, checked 2026-07-20)
- As of 2026-07-20 no GPU host type was offered for Dedicated Host in eu-de. The per-AZ host-type listings returned only s, c and m families, and the service description lists only s, c, m and i families. (KB-TXBP, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- The eu-de DeH listing read on 2026-07-20 held 9 host-type rows: s2, s2-medium, s7n, c3, c7n, c4, m3, m4 and m7n. The listing records what the price API carried on that date, which is separate from whether each host type can be ordered. (KB-UB44, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The Dedicated Host service description (section 3.1.2, revision 17.08.2026) lists more host types than the eu-de API returned on 2026-09-25: s3, c9, c9 Pro, m9, i3, i3 Pro and i3m. Its section 6.2.2 shows s3 and the i3 types for EU-NL only and says the c9 and m9 hosts are offered only on request. (KB-WQWT, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- The TCP service description (section 3.1.2, revision 17.08.2026) lists the Dedicated Host types s2-medium, s2, s3, s7n, c3, c4, c7n, c9, c9 Pro, m3, m4, m7n, m9, i3, i3 Pro and i3m, all of them CPU host types. (KB-ZLGK, contract, checked 2026-09-25)

## cfw (9)

- A standard postPaid Cloud Firewall protecting the EIP of a hub NAT gateway filters spoke egress at the internet border. A top-priority deny rule on one destination address and port blocked exactly that flow while traffic to other destinations still left through the NAT EIP. (KB-437B, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- On 2026-09-25 the Cloud Firewall API and the dedicated WAF API answered in eu-nl as in eu-de: GET /v1/{project_id}/firewall/exist (with offset, limit and service_type) returned the CFW service error CFW.00200005 in both regions, and GET /v1/{project_id}/premium-waf/instance and premium-waf/host returned empty lists in both. The public price API had CFW and dedicated WAF records for eu-de but none for eu-nl. (KB-4MR7, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- POST /v1/{project_id}/acl-rule needs a top-level type field, 0 for the internet border, beside the per-rule fields. Without it the call returns common.00000400, '[type] must not be null'. (KB-75P6, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- A Cloud Firewall instance is created with POST /v2/{project_id}/firewall carrying name, enterprise_project_id, flavor.version standard and charge_info.charge_mode postPaid, and removed with DELETE /v2/{project_id}/firewall/{firewall_id}. (KB-BOFP, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- CFW firewalls are listed with POST /v1/{project_id}/firewalls/list on cfw.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com carrying limit and offset, not with GET. The call returned HTTP 200 and the listed firewall showed is_support_postpaid true. (KB-HJNP, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- Cloud Firewall (CFW) is a cloud-native next-generation firewall that protects the internet border and the VPC borders with real-time intrusion detection and prevention, unified access control, traffic analysis, log audit and tracing. The documentation names access control by 5-tuple, by domain name, by IPS rules in observe or block mode and by IP address groups with blacklists and whitelists, and an HA cluster deployment. Its API answered in eu-de and eu-nl on 2026-09-25 (KB-4MR7). (KB-N7ZV, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- In eu-de the CFW professional edition can be ordered: the user guide says a pay-per-use CFW is created in the professional edition only. The firewalls/list flag is_support_buy_professional was seen true on a test tenant on 2026-07-17; that call is POST only and was not repeated. (KB-NU75, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET /v1/{project_id}/eips/protect?object_id=..&sync=1 must be called once to sync tenant EIPs before POST /v1/{project_id}/eip/protect, otherwise the protect call returns CFW.00200111. Protected objects are listed under .protect_objects in the firewall/exist records, where type 0 is the internet border. (KB-OY5B, live, checked 2026-07-17)
- Cloud Firewall (CFW) protects NAT gateway traffic by protecting the VPC where the NAT gateway resides. CFW professional edition with Enterprise Router (ER) traffic diversion inspects inter-VPC east-west traffic and Direct Connect traffic. (KB-UWHB, docs, checked 2026-07-17)

## aom (8)

- The APM service on eu-de is reached under the endpoint label apm2, not apm. A DNS check on 2026-07-02 showed apm2.eu-de resolving while apm.eu-de did not resolve. (KB-6GFP, live, checked 2026-07-02)
- AOM keeps Prometheus metric data for 30 days: every Prometheus instance listed in eu-de and eu-nl on 2026-09-25 carried compactor_blocks_retention_period 720h and the AOM 2.0 restrictions give a metric storage duration of up to 30 days. This holds although the remote write page says the data can be stored for long. (KB-FVXP, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- AOM 2.0 accepts data from self-built Prometheus servers through remote write into a Prometheus instance: the common instance type is meant for this and the preset default instance also serves it. The setup is an access code under Global Settings, Authentication (at most two per project), then the remote_write block from the instance Settings, Service Addresses appended to prometheus.yml and a restart of Prometheus. (KB-HULX, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- AOM 2.0 is orderable on TCP in eu-de and eu-nl: the service description, revision 17.08.2026, section 6.6.4 lists AOM 2 items. Its user guide is in the docs mirror as of 2026-09-07 while its API reference is still an empty index stub. (KB-I4EH, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- Application Performance Management (APM) monitors the performance of distributed cloud applications in real time and helps operators find faults and performance bottlenecks. An APM agent is deployed with the application without code changes, and APM then collects application metrics, draws the call topology between services, traces requests and finds error or slow URLs; the documentation names Java applications as supported. The service description lists the editions APM Free and APM Enterprise. (KB-P3X6, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- Application Operations Management (AOM) is an operations platform for cloud applications that monitors applications and their cloud resources: the service description names resource monitoring and logging for Cloud Container Engine clusters with log search, event-driven alerts and a visual display of resource utilization, with logs saved in Object Storage Service. The documentation adds association of applications with their resources, metric and alarm analysis and log search filtered by application, host or instance. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for AOM in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-SA7V, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- Self-built Prometheus can also read AOM data back through remote read, with a remote_read block (URL ending in /api/v1/read plus a bearer token) copied from the instance settings. Prometheus adds its external_labels to the search unless filter_external_labels is false, available from Prometheus 2.34. (KB-VBYK, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The remote write URL of an AOM Prometheus instance points to aom-internal-access.<region>.otc.t-systems.com on port 8443 (path /v1/{project_id}/<instance>/push), a private domain name the Prometheus host must resolve. On 2026-09-25 public resolvers answered NXDOMAIN for the eu-de name, which resolved only through the private DNS of the platform, while the eu-nl name resolved publicly. (KB-ZD4H, live, checked 2026-09-25)

## dms (8)

- The DMS documentation handles the move from a single-AZ Kafka instance to a cross-AZ one as a migration to a new instance: create the target with specifications not lower than the original, create the topics with the same settings, then move producers and consumers (production first, production later, or consumption first with a Smart Connect replication task). (KB-5EBZ, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A DMS for Kafka instance is deployed in one AZ or in at least three AZs. Two AZs cannot be selected. (KB-DB6R, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- A DMS for Kafka instance cannot be upgraded to a higher Kafka version after it is created. The documentation says to create another Kafka instance with the higher version instead. (KB-FK4Y, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Distributed Message Service (DMS) is a managed message queuing service: DMS for Kafka provides Kafka instances that are compatible with open-source Apache Kafka and are reached with open-source Kafka clients, with a configurable number of partitions and replicas, as single-broker or cluster flavors of 3 to 50 brokers. Since May 2026 DMS also offers RocketMQ instances as single instance or cluster. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for DMS in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-IXZ3, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- On 2026-09-25 the DMS Kafka product catalog (GET /v2/kafka/products) listed Kafka versions 2.7 and 3.x for new instances in eu-de and eu-nl, and GET /v2/available-zones returned three AZs in each region. The FAQ page of the docs mirror still names v2.3.0 as well. (KB-PBIU, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The AZs of a DMS for Kafka instance cannot be changed after creation, so a single-AZ instance cannot become a multi-AZ instance. To use several AZs you create another instance. (KB-PEZB, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Smart Connect, the DMS function that replicates data between two Kafka instances, is not available for single-node Kafka instances, only for cluster instances. (KB-Q7ZJ, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- In TCP eu-de on 2026-09-25 the classic DMS queue path GET /v1.0/{project_id}/queues returns HTTP 404 and the Kafka instance paths respond normally. The quota paths GET /v2/{project_id}/quotas, documented in the RocketMQ API reference, and GET /v1.0/{project_id}/quotas/dms answer 200 with Kafka and RocketMQ instance quotas. (KB-ZFRE, live, checked 2026-09-25)

## esw (8)

- Three vocabularies name the same three enterprise switch sizes: Basic, Standard and Enhanced on the console and spec page, small, medium and large in the quota table, and esw.small, esw.medium and esw.large as billing SKUs. (KB-4CG4, docs, checked 2026-08-26)
- Enterprise Switch (ES) is a preview service that provides layer 2 communication between the customer's on-premises data center and a VPC, so workloads can move to T Cloud Public (TCP) without changing subnets or IP addresses. It requires a layer 3 connection through Enterprise VPN or Direct Connect, and the documentation describes a layer 2 connection gateway that works with that connection, also where the cloud and on-premises address ranges overlap. (KB-IIT3, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- The ESW documentation requires a service ticket to confirm that a Direct Connect or VPN connection supports VXLAN interconnection with an enterprise switch. The requirement is stated twice. (KB-J7GH, docs, checked 2026-08-26)
- Enterprise Switch (ESW) has no lifecycle API on TCP. Switches and L2 connections are created and changed in the console only. (KB-QDFH, docs, checked 2026-08-26)
- The user guides for enterprise-switch and secure-mail-gateway hold real content while both API references are placeholder pages. Secure Mail Gateway is an outbound mail anti-spam and anti-virus gateway documented as flat pages on intro, security mechanisms, setups and installation. (KB-RHSC, docs, checked 2026-06-08)
- Cloud Eye publishes the namespace SYS.ESW with 6 metrics per instance_id at 1 minute granularity. This is the programmatic read path for an enterprise switch. (KB-VZL5, docs, checked 2026-08-26)
- The ESW forwarding restriction is documented for one direction: untrusted unicast, broadcast and multicast packets (VRRP excepted) and IPv6 packets are not forwarded from the customer data center to the cloud. The cloud to data center direction is not covered by that sentence. (KB-YAWC, docs, checked 2026-08-26)
- The ESW permissions table assigns create, modify, delete and view of a switch or an L2 connection to the VPC Administrator role, and view to Tenant Guest. (KB-YM4F, docs, checked 2026-08-26)

## windows (8)

- A restored Windows server answered RDP after 235 seconds on Server 2019 and 269 seconds on Server 2022 at first boot. A cold rebuild of the same shape took 152 seconds. (KB-2IE5, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- The attack surface reduction rule that blocks credential stealing from LSASS is documented as an equivalent of RunAsPPL LSA Protection rather than a layer added on top of it. (KB-4LLB, docs, checked 2026-06-11)
- Software can be installed on a Windows ECS from an ISO held in OBS: the ISO is pulled into the guest with rclone, mounted with the PowerShell cmdlet Mount-DiskImage -PassThru, installed from the mounted drive letter and released with Dismount-DiskImage. (KB-4VOL, live, checked 2026-06-17)
- LiDAR360 from GreenValley uses a node-locked license key with online activation through its License Manager. The key binds to the machine, and moving it requires a return or deactivation on the old machine before activation on the new one. (KB-7DW6, live, checked 2026-06-17)
- On Windows Server 2025 an ECS that is reachable only over RDP cannot complete a forced first-logon password change. The server requires NLA by default and the client stops with error 0x1207, password must be changed before logging on. (KB-EDZM, live, checked 2026-06-11)
- A restored Windows server brings its data disk up Offline because of the SAN policy OfflineShared. Setting san policy=OnlineAll before the full-ECS image is created fixes this once in the image instead of at each restore. (KB-F535, docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- Client Windows images such as Windows 10 and Windows 7 may only be used to create ECS on a Dedicated Host. The service description additionally limits bring-your-own Microsoft Windows and SQL Server licences to Physical and Dedicated Hosts. (KB-O4J5, contract, checked 2026-06-10)
- Windows Server 2022 and Windows Server 2025 are offered as public images and run on the pi5e GPU family with NVIDIA L4. (KB-X52S, live, checked 2026-09-25)

## dcs (7)

- After an online migration between DCS Redis 4.0 or later instances, DCS can swap the IP addresses of source and target so clients keep their address, with a rollback of the swap possible. It works for single-node, master/standby, read/write splitting and Proxy Cluster instances with the same port, password and whitelist, while the instances turn read-only for one minute during the swap. (KB-2XZS, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The Redis major version of a DCS instance cannot be changed after creation, for example from 5.0 to 6.0. The documented path is a new instance with the higher version and a data migration into it. (KB-5XW5, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Distributed Cache Service (DCS) is a managed in-memory NoSQL database based on Redis, with tools for automatic provisioning, maintenance, monitoring, backup and recovery. Instances are single node, primary/standby with 2 to 5 replicas, or cluster, plus read/write splitting and proxy cluster types; new instances run Redis 4.x, 5.x, 6.x or 7.x, and the documentation states that primary/standby and cluster instances can be deployed across availability zones. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for DCS in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-AS3U, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- New Redis 3.x instances of DCS can no longer be booked, while existing Redis 3.x instances can still be used as usual. (KB-P4WZ, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- DCS migrates data between instances either online (in full or incrementally, when the source allows SYNC and PSYNC) or by importing AOF or RDB backup files. Every DCS instance type can be the source for every DCS target type. (KB-QQFN, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On 2026-09-25 the DCS flavor list in eu-de and eu-nl tagged the single-node, master/standby and Redis Cluster flavors with Redis 4.0, 5.0, 6.0 and 7.0. Proxy Cluster and read/write splitting flavors carried 4.0, 5.0 and 6.0, so a Redis 6.0 target exists in all five instance types. (KB-TUIU, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- In place, DCS upgrades only the minor or proxy version of Redis 4.0 and later instances, always to the latest one, without rollback and with connection address, password, whitelist and alarms unchanged. During the upgrade a migrating shard turns read-only for one minute and clients are intermittently disconnected. (KB-VP4J, docs, checked 2026-09-25)

## dds (7)

- The DDS lifecycle table gives 4.0, 4.2 and 4.4 planned end of marketing dates that have passed (January 2022, November 2022 and September 2023) and plans their end of service for April 2027, April 2028 and April 2029. It gives no dates for 5.0 and marks the older 3.2 and 3.4 unavailable. (KB-4V3I, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The document database DDS offers the versions 4.0, 4.2, 4.4 and 5.0 in eu-de and eu-nl: on 2026-09-25 GET /v3/{project_id}/datastores/DDS-Community/versions returned exactly these four in both regions. (KB-C2YH, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- DDS 4.2, 4.4 and 5.0 run the RocksDB storage engine, while DDS 4.0 runs WiredTiger. (KB-JP6W, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- DDS is reached with a client driver compatible with MongoDB 3.0 or later. For the distributed transactions of DDS 4.2 and later the version table notes that the MongoDB drivers must be updated for MongoDB 4.2. (KB-ML3K, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- DDS is a MongoDB-compatible service, not the MongoDB community engine itself: DDS 4.0 (since 16 October 2018) and DDS 4.2 and later use community version 4.0.3 as their baseline and add independently developed features. A DDS version number states compatibility and does not correspond to a community release. (KB-MNLG, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The DDS docs offer version 5.0 only as replica set or cluster, while 4.0, 4.2 and 4.4 also come as single node. The live flavor list of 2026-09-25 does not show this limit: in eu-de and eu-nl it tags all 21 flavors, the five single node flavors included, with 4.0, 4.2, 4.4 and 5.0 (creating a 5.0 single node was not tried). (KB-OKUT, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Document Database Service (DDS) is a managed document-oriented NoSQL database compatible with MongoDB, with a high availability architecture, disaster recovery failover, online scaling, backup and restore. It is deployed as a single node, as a three-node replica set with automatic failover, or as a sharded cluster with config nodes, shards and mongos nodes. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for DDS in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-OUE6, contract, checked 2026-10-11)

## hss (7)

- On a CCE worker node the HSS host agent runs three processes: hostguard at about 311 MB RSS, containerserver at about 42 MB and hostwatch at about 12 MB, roughly 365 MB RSS together, about 563 MB on disk and about 1.5 percent CPU at idle. Measured on EulerOS 2.9. (KB-2ZZR, live, checked 2026-02-04)
- Host Security Service (HSS) protects server workloads: it identifies and manages the assets on servers, reduces risks and defends against intrusions and web page tampering. It includes Container Guard Service (CGS), which scans container images for vulnerabilities and configuration issues, and Web Tamper Protection (WTP), which monitors website directories in real time and restores tampered files from backups. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the HSS API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-3CST, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- The HSS user manual documents an HSS Administrator role and an HSSFullAccess policy for HSS permissions. Whether a subscribed domain needs them in addition to the subscription was not tested. (KB-AVCB, docs, checked 2026-09-02)
- On 2026-09-25 the documented HSS v5 API was not published in eu-nl: four GET paths answered 404 APIGW.0101 there, while in eu-de they reached the service (403 for a user without HSS rights). The public price API listed only the HSS Premium Edition for eu-nl, although section 6.10.7 of the service description lists four editions for EU-NL. (KB-OVOE, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On 2026-09-25 in eu-de, GET under hss.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com/v5/{project_id} with the test key answered HTTP 403 APIGW.0302 (IAM user not authorized) for host-management/hosts, vulnerability/hosts, baseline/risk-configs, event/events and billing/quotas-detail, and HTTP 404 APIGW.0101 for asset/processes, asset/ports, asset/softwares, asset/accounts, asset/users and policy/groups. The HTTP 200 answers seen on 2026-02-04 on a premium-edition tenant were not reproduced. (KB-T4OL, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On 2026-02-04 on a premium-edition HSS tenant, GET on the HSS v5 paths antivirus, webtamper/static/policy and image/build-history returned HTTP 404. (KB-UX32, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- On 2026-09-02 in eu-de, GET /v5/{project_id}/host-management/hosts returned 403 APIGW.0302, and billing/quotas-detail, policy/groups, asset/users and host-management/groups returned 404 APIGW.0101, with AK/SK signing and with a project-scoped token. The same access key received 200 from ECS. (KB-UXVH, live, checked 2026-09-02)

## ms-licensing (7)

- The Microsoft clause of the T Cloud Public (TCP) service description names Windows Server 2016, 2019 and 2022 as the sublicensed versions. (KB-6EWX, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- A full-text search of the T Cloud Public (TCP) service description, revision 17.08.2026, for remote desktop, terminal, multi-session and session host returned zero hits. The platform documentation mentions the Remote Desktop Session Host role only in RDP troubleshooting FAQs for Windows ECS, for example removing the role when the 120-day licensing grace period ends. (KB-DXKH, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- Self-managed SQL Server under bring-your-own-license runs on Dedicated Host or physical servers. (KB-PWIV, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- Remote Desktop Services client access licences (RDS CAL) and SAL subscriptions are not price-list items on T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de and have to be brought from outside the platform. (KB-RBRL, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The service description, section 3.1.4.2 in revision 17.08.2026, limits customer-owned Microsoft Windows Server and Microsoft SQL Server licences to Physical and Dedicated Hosts. (KB-SOBG, contract, checked 2026-08-24)
- The ECS price listing (sn=ecs) of eu-de carried six operating system tiers on each of its 66 flavors on 2026-09-24: Open Linux, Oracle Linux, RedHat Linux, SUSE Linux, SUSE for SAP and Windows. None of the six ECS service names carried a SQL Server line; SQL Server appears under rds and ms-man-db. (KB-WFB7, live, checked 2026-09-24)
- Bring-your-own Microsoft SQL Server licenses are accepted only on Dedicated Hosts (DeH) or physical servers, so a self-managed SQL Server on ECS under BYOL needs one of those. (KB-XQQY, contract, checked 2026-09-25)

## swr (7)

- Software Repository for Containers (SWR) is a managed private container image registry: container images are uploaded, downloaded and removed through the console or the Docker CLI. The documentation adds private repositories with read, write and edit permissions per user, use together with Cloud Container Engine or on its own, and faster image pulls for CCE clusters. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for SWR in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-6MXP, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- The SWR organisation quota is 5 per account and is read at GET /v2/manage/projects/{project_id}/quotas. (KB-7OJD, live, checked 2026-07-02)
- The SWR feature gates read on eu-de on 2026-09-25 returned enable_image_scan false, enable_hss_service false, enable_cci_service false and enable_image_sync false, so image vulnerability scanning and the SWR to CCI hookup were switched off for that account. (KB-MWNP, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET /v2/manage/projects/{project_id}/feature-gates on swr-api.eu-de.otc.t-systems.com returns per-domain service enablement flags, for example enable_hss_service, which show whether the API gateway lets a service through for that domain. (KB-OIJ5, live, checked 2026-09-02)
- SWR accepts two docker login forms. A temporary secret valid 24 hours comes from POST /v2/manage/utils/secret and arrives in the X-Swr-Dockerlogin response header. A long term login valid one year uses the region name plus the access key as user name and an HMAC-SHA256 of the access key, keyed with the secret key, as password. (KB-R2VK, live, checked 2026-07-02)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) offers a private container registry in Software Repository for Containers (SWR): container images are pushed, pulled and deleted with the Docker CLI or the console in the customer's own namespaces and repositories. Docker login with a temporary secret or a long-term login derived from an access key was tested live in eu-de (KB-R2VK). (KB-U7YD, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- SWR management calls run against the swr-api host on eu-de. GET /v2/manage/repos, GET /v2/manage/namespaces and GET /v2/manage/projects/{project_id}/feature-gates each answered HTTP 200. Image pull and push use the separate swr registry host. (KB-ZG4G, live, checked 2026-07-02)

## gaussdb (6)

- TaurusDB is not available in eu-nl: on 2026-09-25 its API answered HTTP 404 APIGW.0101 on the eu-nl endpoint, while the endpoint list names TaurusDB for eu-de only. (KB-7Y6M, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- GeminiDB is a managed cloud-native NoSQL database with decoupled compute and storage that is compatible with Apache Cassandra and InfluxDB. GeminiDB for Cassandra supports the Cassandra Query Language (CQL) and the InfluxDB-compatible variant serves time-series data; the documentation describes deployment of nodes across three availability zones, backup and restore, and monitoring with alarms. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the GeminiDB API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-FJQ6, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- TaurusDB is compatible with MySQL 8.0 and decouples compute from storage: one instance holds up to 128 TB and consists of one primary node and up to 15 read replicas on the same shared storage. No storage size is set when an instance is created. (KB-MNIA, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- TaurusDB is a managed distributed relational database for enterprise requirements that is compatible with MySQL (MySQL 8.0 per the engine table) and decouples compute from storage for high scalability and storage capacity. An instance has one primary (write) node and several read replicas on shared storage, and the documentation gives up to 128 TB of storage per instance. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the TaurusDB API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-RVSG, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- In eu-de TaurusDB offered engine version 8.0 (MySQL 8.0.22) on 2026-09-25 with six x86 flavors from 2 vCPU and 8 GB to 64 vCPU and 512 GB RAM. The flavors were on sale in eu-de-01 and eu-de-03 and unsupported in eu-de-02. (KB-UN55, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- DRS migrates MySQL 5.5, 5.6, 5.7 and 8.0 databases from RDS for MySQL, ECS, on-premises or other clouds to TaurusDB cluster instances in real time. (KB-YL3W, docs, checked 2026-09-25)

## bms (5)

- Bare Metal Server (BMS) is ramped down on TCP. Its user guide and API reference remain in the documentation mirror, so documentation presence is no proof that a service is orderable. (KB-DBAV, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- Bare Metal Server (BMS) is ramped down and no longer orderable in eu-de, while its user guide and API reference remain in the documentation: on 2026-09-25 its three flavors were abandon or sellout in every AZ. The six rows under the bms key of the price API are the Physical ECS row BM General Purpose c7t.28xl and five EVS rows, not BMS flavors. (KB-PYYO, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- BMS bare metal is not orderable in eu-de. The service description revision 17.08.2026 carries no BMS entry and offers bare metal as Physical ECS c7t, with physical c9 and m9 announced from 01.10.2026 on request; on 2026-09-25 the BMS flavor listing marked every AZ of the three returned flavors as abandon or sellout. (KB-UEVJ, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- The service description revision 17.08.2026 offers bare metal as Physical ECS: BM General Purpose c7t.28xl, EU-DE only (section 3.1.1.2 and section 6.2.1.19), and the ECS flavor c7t.28xlarge.4.physical reported eu-de-03(normal) on 2026-09-25. Physical c9 and m9 flavors are announced from 01.10.2026 and offered only on request. (KB-WD2H, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- The opentelekomcloud provider 1.37.8 ships 330 resources. Compared with 1.36.64 it adds 15 resources and drops the two BMS resources compute_bms_server_v2 and compute_bms_tags_v2. (KB-WMZI, live, checked 2026-09-16)

## dws (5)

- In DWS, CREATE EXTERNAL SCHEMA and ALTER EXTERNAL SCHEMA point at a metastore: source_type accepts dli, lakeformation or hive. The syntax is available from DWS 8.3.0. (KB-DB4T, docs, checked 2026-07-29)
- A DWS foreign table on OBS reads ORC, TEXT, JSON, CSV, CarbonData, Parquet and Hudi, and writes ORC and Parquet. Iceberg appears in neither list. (KB-JZIX, docs, checked 2026-07-29)
- Data Warehouse Service (DWS) is a managed online analytical database for searching and analyzing large data sets on a distributed architecture. The documentation states that it supports ANSI/ISO SQL-92, SQL-99 and SQL:2003 syntax and is interoperable with the PostgreSQL, Oracle, Teradata and MySQL ecosystems. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the DWS API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-MQCW, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- The DWS flavor dws.m3.xlarge in eu-de carries minAmount 3 in the price API, so the smallest cluster of that flavor is three nodes that run whether or not a query is issued. (KB-TP5W, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The TCP service description, revision 17.08.2026, section 6.9.2, prices DWS nodes for EU-DE only and marks every EU-NL price column n.a., so DWS is not offered in eu-nl under that contract revision. (KB-XFT6, contract, checked 2026-10-05)

## modelarts (5)

- The ModelArts v2 training job listing is POST /v2/{project_id}/training-job-searches, not a GET. GET /v1/{project_id}/services answers HTTP 200 with a count field and serves, like the flavor and engine GETs, as a cheap liveness probe. (KB-FFU5, live, checked 2026-07-02)
- ModelArts is an end-to-end AI development platform that covers data preprocessing and semi-automatic labeling, distributed training, automated model building and deployment of models on T Cloud Public (TCP). The documentation names the open-source frameworks TensorFlow, PyTorch and MindSpore, dedicated resources and custom images for custom frameworks. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the ModelArts API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-O2UG, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- GET /v1/{project_id}/services/specifications returns 5 inference specifications in eu-de: cpu.2u, high.p3 without a GPU, bm.gpu.v100 with one V100 32GB, high.p2 with an 8GB GPU and gpu.t4u8 with a T4 16GB. Their state moves between normal and sellout: on 2026-09-25 two checks disagreed on cpu.2u and high.p3, while high.p2 and gpu.t4u8 were sellout in every read. Sellout marks a specification the listing returns while no capacity is on offer. (KB-Q6XQ, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- The ModelArts endpoint on eu-de resolves and answers AK/SK signed calls. GET /v2/{project_id}/training-job-flavors returned 3 training flavors on 2026-07-02: modelarts.vm.cpu.2u, modelarts.vm.gpu.v100 with one V100 32GB and modelarts.vm.gpu.4v100 with four V100 32GB, each with max_num 1, so single node training. (KB-TOS5, live, checked 2026-09-25)
- GET /v2/{project_id}/training-job-engines returned 2 preset engines on eu-de on 2026-09-25: pytorch_1.8.0-cuda_10.2-py_3.7-ubuntu_18.04-x86_64 and tensorflow_2.1.0-cuda_10.1-py_3.7-ubuntu_18.04-x86_64. (KB-WMZT, live, checked 2026-09-25)

## rds-mysql (5)

- An RDS for MySQL instance can be stopped for at most seven days. If it is not started manually within that time, RDS starts it again automatically in the next maintenance window. (KB-7R4J, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- The stop call POST /v3/{project_id}/instances/{instance_id}/action/shutdown is documented for pay-per-use RDS instances. While an instance is stopped the ECS part stops being billed, but EIPs, storage and backups are still billed. (KB-PF2Q, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Stopping a primary RDS instance also stops its read replicas, and a read replica cannot be stopped on its own. (KB-Q5RG, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- On 2026-09-16 the RDS datastore listing in eu-de returned mysql 8.4, 8.0 and 5.7, and postgresql 18, 17, 16, 15, 14 and 13. (KB-TMQ5, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- RDS for MySQL is capped at 4 TB of storage: the service description gives RDS disks of 40 GiB to 4 TiB and the RDS user guide limits storage scaling and autoscaling of MySQL instances to 4,000 GB. (KB-UFQA, contract, checked 2026-09-25)

## vpcep (5)

- A VPC endpoint for an interface endpoint service can get the same IPv4 address in a target project, since the address can be set by hand when the endpoint is created there, which needs a subnet in the target VPC whose CIDR contains that address. (KB-DMY4, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- Rebuilding a workload in another project includes creating the VPC endpoint services and the endpoints again in that project. (KB-ESOO, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- VPC Endpoint (VPCEP) provides private connections from a VPC to VPC endpoint services, which are cloud services such as OBS and DNS or the customer's own private services, with no Elastic IP, NAT gateway or VPN connection needed. On-premises networks attached over VPN or Direct Connect can reach those services through a VPC endpoint as well. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for VPCEP in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-LHEC, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- The resource opentelekomcloud_vpcep_endpoint_v1 takes the network id of the subnet in the argument subnet_id. (KB-N5BP, live, checked 2026-09-16)
- On T Cloud Public (TCP) in eu-de VPC endpoints are listed with GET /v1/{project_id}/vpc-endpoints on the VPCEP service endpoint. The response returns them under the key endpoints, each entry carrying id, endpoint_service_name, status, ip and vpc_id. (KB-WQHX, docs, checked 2026-09-25)

## container (4)

- The Neutron port API on TCP deviates from upstream OpenStack, so the OpenShift Machine API fails to create network ports when a MachineSet scales workers. The documented workaround replaces machine-api-provider-openstack with a patched image published in SWR. The deviation affects OpenStack-native tooling in general. (KB-5ZKF, docs, checked 2026-06-08)
- OpenShift is orderable on TCP through the Cloud Create self-service catalogue as self-managed Red Hat OpenShift Container Platform, bring your own subscription, installed UPI on the OpenStack-compatible API. Template versions 4.16.19 and 4.18.30 are offered, further versions on request. (KB-6EFK, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- UCS on T Cloud Public (TCP) is domain-scoped. GET /v1/clusters on the eu-de UCS endpoint answered HTTP 200 with an empty item list when signed with a domain-scoped IAM token, and answered HTTP 401 UCS.01000003 when signed with project-scoped AK/SK. (KB-L6UF, live, checked 2026-09-01)
- A Cloud Create OpenShift deployment builds 3 master nodes, at least 2 workers, a bootstrap node that is deleted after installation and a bastion. It needs about 30 minutes or more until the cluster is ready. (KB-UE2E, docs, checked 2026-06-08)

## lts (4)

- Log Tank Service (LTS) is a central cloud logging service that stores log data from many sources in one place and forwards it to Object Storage Service or Distributed Message Service for further processing or archiving. The documentation names real-time collection of host and cloud service logs, keyword and fuzzy log search, alarm reporting and a retention period the customer sets, after which logs are deleted unless transferred to OBS for long-term storage. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for LTS in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-3YYI, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- GET /v2/{project_id}/groups on the LTS endpoint answers HTTP 200 with a log_groups array. Each entry carries log_group_id, log_group_name and ttl_in_days. (KB-CRKU, live, checked 2026-07-02)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) collects logs centrally in Log Tank Service (LTS): host and cloud service logs are gathered in real time into log groups and streams, searched by keyword, used for alarms, kept for a retention period of 1 to 365 days per log group (KB-XLF6) and transferred to OBS or DMS for longer storage. (KB-MZQU, docs, checked 2026-10-11)
- LTS log group retention is set per group through ttl_in_days and runs from 1 to 365 days, with 30 days as the default. (KB-XLF6, live, checked 2026-07-02)

## cci (3)

- A CCI API that answers on eu-de is not proof that CCI can be ordered for production use. The service description, revision 17.08.2026, lists CCI in section 3.12 among the preview and beta versions for testing purposes only, and the CCI user manual still marks it as beta. (KB-KA35, contract, checked 2026-09-25)
- Cloud Container Instance (CCI) is a serverless container engine in preview that runs containers without creating or managing server clusters; pods get CPU, memory and, depending on the pod type, GPU. As a preview version it is meant for testing and Telekom advises against live use (see KB-KA35). The Regions and Endpoints page lists the CCI API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-SNAP, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- On the CCI endpoint the paths / and /healthz answered HTTP 404 with error code APIGW.0101, so only the published API paths are reachable through the gateway. (KB-XNOI, live, checked 2026-07-02)

## css (3)

- CSS on eu-de offers three Elasticsearch versions: 7.6.2, 7.9.3 and 7.10.2. (KB-5FFD, live, checked 2026-10-05)
- Cloud Search Service (CSS) is a managed search and analytics service that supports the Elasticsearch protocol, with tools for automatic provisioning, scaling, maintenance, monitoring, backup and recovery. An instance runs in standalone mode or as a cluster of 3 to 32 nodes; the documentation describes Elasticsearch and OpenSearch clusters with Kibana or OpenSearch Dashboards built in. The Regions and Endpoints page lists the CSS API endpoint in eu-de. (KB-TUGV, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- CSS ships its own vector engine: index setting index.vector true, field type vector, a query.vector DSL with topk and pre-filtering, and seven index algorithms (FLAT, GRAPH, GRAPH_PQ, GRAPH_SQ8, GRAPH_SQ4, IVF_GRAPH, IVF_GRAPH_PQ). The version table marks it supported on ES 7.6.2 and 7.10.2. (KB-U434, docs, checked 2026-08-27)

## sfs (3)

- Scalable File Service (SFS) provides scalable shared file storage based on NFSv3 inside the customer's tenant. It is offered as SFS General Purpose with volumes up to 4 PB and as SFS Turbo in Standard, Performance and HPC types (HPC 20 to HPC 1000) from 500 GiB up to 1 PB. The Regions and Endpoints page lists an SFS endpoint in eu-de and an SFS Turbo endpoint in eu-nl. (KB-4I6S, contract, checked 2026-10-11)
- Scalable File Service on T Cloud Public (TCP) offers NFSv3 access only. SFS Capacity-Oriented, SFS Turbo and General Purpose file systems expose no SMB or CIFS share, and general purpose file systems cannot be mounted to Windows servers. (KB-7TPW, docs, checked 2026-08-24)
- T Cloud Public (TCP) offers shared file storage over NFS: Scalable File Service (SFS) General Purpose and SFS Turbo file systems are accessed over NFSv3 only, without SMB or CIFS shares (KB-7TPW). (KB-TX7A, contract, checked 2026-10-11)

## dr (2)

- The German twin-core sites are Magdeburg and Biere in Saxony-Anhalt, about 18 km apart. The Dutch region is near Amsterdam, about 550 km away. A separate Swiss offering exists near Bern. (KB-A2NM, docs, checked 2026-07-09)
- For Windows recovery, starting a prepared stopped ECS took 23 s plus about 43 s per data disk attached sequentially, while rebuilding from a backup took 11 to 19 s for the image plus 149 s to RDP with data disks included. The two meet at about three data disks. (KB-B4LD, live, checked 2026-09-02)

## linux (2)

- scp to MRS nodes fails with 'subsystem request failed on channel 0' because the sftp subsystem is disabled. Copy files by piping over ssh instead: ssh host "cat > /path" < file. (KB-23NN, live, checked 2026-07-28)
- On MRS nodes, sourcing /opt/Bigdata/client/bigdata_env under set -u aborts the script silently because bigdata_env references unset variables. Use set -o pipefail alone. (KB-CEIK, live, checked 2026-07-28)

## tender (2)

- TCP is listed as certified for ISO/IEC 27001, 27017, 27018 and 27701, plus ISO 9001, 14001, 20000-1, 22301, 50001, 45001 and 22237. (KB-R3TV, docs, checked 2026-09-25)
- KRITIS status for TCP is a registration with the German federal office for information security, not an issued certificate. (KB-TQFH, docs, checked 2026-10-08)

## as (1)

- Auto Scaling (AS) is a service that automatically scales Elastic Cloud Server instances and bandwidth out and in by conditions the customer sets. Scaling is triggered by alarm-based policies on Cloud Eye metrics such as vCPU, memory, disk or inbound traffic, by scheduled policies or by periodic policies, and AS works together with Elastic Load Balancer, Elastic Volume Service and Elastic IP. The Regions and Endpoints page lists API endpoints for AS in eu-de and eu-nl. (KB-4COH, contract, checked 2026-10-11)

## identity (1)

- Enterprise projects cover 46 service providers in eu-de on 2026-09-25, among them vpc, ecs, evs, rds, obs, elb, kms, cce, css, dws, gaussdb, waf, CFW, cbr, lts, ces, ims, bms, deh, sfs, dli, er, dds, mrs, vpn and APIG. The hss provider is no longer in the list. (KB-SZLC, live, checked 2026-09-25)

## monitoring (1)

- GET /v2/{project_id}/fgs/functions/{urn}/statistics/{period} returns arrays for count, fail_count, duration, max_duration, min_duration, concurrency_num, instance_num and reject_count. The accepted periods are 5, 15 and 60. (KB-DE5J, live, checked 2026-07-30)
